**Implemented controls:** Socket message handler input validation, File upload size and type restrictions, Client-side input validation.

**Assessment:** Server-side validation is handler-local. Wallet addresses and socket payloads reach in-memory state unchecked (🟡 [F-125](#f-125)), the chat route has no input or volume limit (🟠 [F-049](#f-049)), and the wallet top-up amount is unbounded (🟡 [F-077](#f-077)). Upload limits and client validators exist but are partial.

**Findings in this category without a control:**

- 🟠 [F-049](#f-049) — Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint
- 🟡 [F-077](#f-077) — Wallet top-up amount accepted without payment or bounds
- 🟡 [F-125](#f-125) — Unbounded in-memory wallet set growth

<a id="validation-approach"></a>
#### 6.6.1 Validation Approach

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - checks are handler-local, and the inspected handlers show no schema validation.

Validation is spread across handlers: size and MIME limits on uploads, an `Array` check in socket handlers and Angular form validators in the client.

**Security assessment**

No shared validation layer was evidenced:

- Chat requests are unbounded (🟠 [F-049](#f-049)).
- Wallet top-up accepts any amount (🟡 [F-077](#f-077)).
- Wallet addresses grow an in-memory set without limits (🟡 [F-125](#f-125)).

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="request-input-validation-for-wallet-addresses"></a>
#### 6.6.2 Request input validation for wallet addresses

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - `walletAddress` goes into a Set with no type, format or length check.

This control is not evidenced in the repository.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): walletAddress is added to the Set without type, format or length checks. The source shows no size cap on walletsConnected, so repeated requests can grow memory. -->
Repeated requests can grow memory:

- No format check on the address and no size cap on `walletsConnected` (🟡 [F-125](#f-125)).

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="socket-message-handler-input-validation"></a>
#### 6.6.3 Socket message handler input validation

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - handlers take untyped client data with no schema validation or rate limit.

`registerWebsocketEvents.ts` registers `verify*` and `notification received` handlers. Payloads are typed `any` and evaluated against challenge-solving conditions.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): Handlers take client-supplied data typed as any. They do no schema validation. 'notification received' removes a shared notification by flag match, and any client can trigger this without authorization or ownership checks. The other handlers only evaluate the payload against challenge-solving conditions, with optional chaining and an Array check. They have no rate limiting. -->
Handlers act on client data without validation or ownership checks:

- Any client can remove a shared notification by flag match.
- Client-reported events mark challenges solved (🟡 [F-065](#f-065)).
- Unauthenticated events alter global state (🟡 [F-091](#f-091)).

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="file-upload-size-and-type-restrictions"></a>
#### 6.6.4 File upload size and type restrictions

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - size and MIME limits exist, but the MIME type is client-supplied.

The memory-storage upload has a 200 KB limit. The disk upload checks the MIME type against a png/jpg allowlist and applies `sanitizeFilename`.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): The memory-storage upload has a 200 KB limit. The disk upload checks the MIME type against a png/jpg allowlist and applies sanitizeFilename. The MIME type is client-supplied. The upload checks for /file-upload (checkFileType and checkUploadSize) were not inspected. -->
Limits are present but do not cover what the content does:

- Zip extraction allows writes outside the target directory (🟠 [F-024](#f-024)).
- Remote image bodies are stored unchecked (🟡 [F-081](#f-081)).
- XML and YAML parsing blocks the event loop (🟡 [F-126](#f-126)).
- The `/file-upload` checks were not inspected.

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="client-side-input-validation"></a>
#### 6.6.5 Client-side input validation

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - client checks sanitise guest basket data but are only a usability aid.

Angular parses guest basket data from `sessionStorage` and enforces integer product IDs and a minimum quantity. Login form validators require a non-empty value.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): Guest basket data from sessionStorage is parsed and sanitised: integer product IDs and a minimum quantity are enforced. Login form validators only require a non-empty value. The client check is a usability aid and cannot replace server-side validation. -->
Client validation can be skipped by calling the API directly:

- Duplicate JSON keys bypass the basket ownership check on the server (🟡 [F-133](#f-133)).
- Wallet top-up amounts are not bounded server-side (🟡 [F-077](#f-077)).

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

### 6.7 Output Encoding and Rendering Controls


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-007](#w-007), [W-030](#w-030)
**Verdict:** 🟠 Weak

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the section's default mechanism — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.7.1 Output Encoding and Client-Side Rendering](#671-output-encoding-and-client-side-rendering)

**Implemented controls:** Angular default template escaping for ordinary bindings.

**Assessment:** Escaping works until a component opts out. Several components mark stored or query-derived content as trusted HTML (🟠 [F-032](#f-032), 🟠 [F-036](#f-036), 🟡 [F-089](#f-089)) and the server compiles a stored username into a template (🟠 [F-030](#f-030)). Reflected XSS appears on the order tracking page (🟠 [F-038](#f-038)).

**Findings in this category without a control:**

- 🟠 [F-037](#f-037) — DOM XSS
- 🟠 [F-038](#f-038) — Reflected XSS
- 🟠 [F-039](#f-039) — Reflected XSS
- 🟡 [F-078](#f-078) — Stored XSS
- 🟡 [F-088](#f-088) — Stored XSS
- 🟡 [F-089](#f-089) — Product descriptions marked as trusted HTML

<a id="output-encoding-and-client-side-rendering"></a>
#### 6.7.1 Output Encoding and Client-Side Rendering

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - Angular escaping is bypassed where components mark content as trusted HTML.

Output encoding stops user-supplied text from running as script in other users' pages. Most values render as plain text, but several pages insert stored and query-derived content as HTML.

**Security assessment**

Several pages render user-controlled content as HTML. Stored XSS appears in feedback comments (🟠 [F-032](#f-032)), the administration view (🟠 [F-033](#f-033), 🟠 [F-034](#f-034), 🟠 [F-035](#f-035)) and the last-login view (🟡 [F-088](#f-088)), with DOM and reflected variants in search and tracking (🟠 [F-036](#f-036), 🟠 [F-037](#f-037), 🟠 [F-038](#f-038), 🟠 [F-039](#f-039)). The components call `DomSanitizer` trust methods and bind the result with `[innerHTML]`.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-032](#f-032) — Stored XSS from feedback comments on the About page
- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — Stored XSS from user emails
- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Stored XSS
- 🟠 [F-035](#f-035) — Stored XSS
- 🟠 [F-036](#f-036) — DOM-based XSS

#### 6.8.1 Socket\.IO CORS origin restriction

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - a single fixed origin, which is a development value.

`Socket.IO` CORS is limited to one fixed origin, `http://localhost:4200`.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): CORS is limited to a single fixed origin, http://localhost:4200. This is a development value and may not match a deployed origin. CORS is a browser-enforced control, so non-browser clients are not constrained by it. It is not a substitute for authentication. -->
CORS narrows browser access but is not authentication:

- The origin is a development value that may not match a deployed one.
- Non-browser clients ignore CORS, and the channel is unauthenticated (🟡 [F-093](#f-093)).
- Cookie-authenticated profile updates lack CSRF protection (🟡 [F-080](#f-080)).

### 6.9 Cryptography Secrets and Data Protection


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-008](#w-008), [W-047](#w-047)
**Verdict:** 🟠 Weak

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.9.1 Password Hashing and Credential Storage](#691-password-hashing-and-credential-storage)
- [6.9.2 Embedded SQLite database access protection](#692-embedded-sqlite-database-access-protection)

**Implemented controls:** Password hashing, Embedded SQLite database access protection.

**Assessment:** Secrets live in source: signing and HMAC keys (🟠 [F-015](#f-015)), the cookie secret (🟡 [F-111](#f-111)) and cleartext account passwords (🟡 [F-121](#f-121)). Passwords are unsalted `MD5` (🟠 [F-048](#f-048) (Passwords stored as unsalted `MD5` hashes)), and TOTP secrets (🟡 [F-120](#f-120)) and card numbers (🟡 [F-123](#f-123)) are stored unencrypted.

**Findings in this category without a control:**

- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Hardcoded JWT signing private key and HMAC keys in source
- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — JWT signing private key is embedded in source code
- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token
- 🟡 [F-079](#f-079) — Forgeable continue codes
- 🟡 [F-111](#f-111) — Hardcoded cookie signing secret in server setup
- 🟡 [F-115](#f-115) — Hardcoded test account credentials in client bundle
- 🟡 [F-116](#f-116) — Hardcoded test account credentials in the client bundle
- 🟡 [F-120](#f-120) — TOTP secrets stored in plaintext in the Users table
- 🟡 [F-121](#f-121) — Cleartext account passwords embedded in login handler
- 🟡 [F-124](#f-124) — TOTP secrets stored in plaintext user column

<a id="password-hashing"></a><a id="password-hashing-and-credential-storage"></a>
#### 6.9.1 Password Hashing and Credential Storage

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - unsalted `MD5` with no work factor.

Passwords are hashed with `MD5` through a shared helper, and the User model setter applies the same digest before storage.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): Passwords are hashed with unsalted MD5, which is fast and vulnerable to rainbow-table and brute-force attacks. A hardcoded HMAC key is also present at line 42. -->
Two independent weaknesses sit on password storage:

- Unsalted `MD5` is fast to brute-force and rainbow-table (🟠 [F-048](#f-048) — Passwords stored as unsalted MD5 hashes (Passwords stored as unsalted `MD5` hashes), 🟡 [F-112](#f-112) — Passwords verified and stored as unsalted fast).
- Hash exposure is easy: the whoami route and reset response return it (🟡 [F-099](#f-099), 🟠 [F-046](#f-046)).
- A hardcoded HMAC key also sits in the same file (🟠 [F-015](#f-015)).

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="embedded-sqlite-database-access-protection"></a>
#### 6.9.2 Embedded SQLite database access protection

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - a file-based database with no encryption at rest or database-level access control.

Sequelize connects to the file-based SQLite database `data/juiceshop.sqlite` using placeholder credentials that SQLite ignores.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): Sequelize connects to a file-based SQLite database (data/juiceshop.sqlite) using placeholder credentials; SQLite has no login of its own. No encryption at rest or database-level access control is visible in this file, so protection relies on filesystem permissions and the application process boundary. Only the connection setup was inspected; file permissions and deployment hardening were not. -->
Protection relies on filesystem permissions and the process:

- Sensitive columns are stored unencrypted (🟡 [F-123](#f-123), 🟡 [F-124](#f-124), 🟡 [F-120](#f-120)).
- File permissions and deployment hardening were not inspected.

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="outbound-websocket-rpc-provider-handling-and-error-reset"></a>
#### 6.10.1 Outbound WebSocket RPC provider handling and error reset

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - a fixed `wss://` endpoint over TLS, but failed connections are not cleaned up.

<!-- Model implementation note (facts for the intro): routes/nftMint.ts lines 16-30; routes/web3Wallet.ts lines 18-33 -->
`routes/nftMint.ts` and `routes/web3Wallet.ts` open an ethers WebSocket provider to a fixed `wss://` URL. The API key comes from an environment variable.

The diagram shows the intended provider connection and event listener setup:
```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    participant API as Wallet Route
    participant RPC as RPC Provider
    API->>RPC: Open WebSocket with API key
    RPC-->>API: Contract events
    API->>API: Record minters or exploiters
```

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): The RPC endpoint is a fixed wss:// URL, so users cannot steer the destination, and TLS is used. The API key comes from an environment variable, not source. An empty key is the fallback. The error handler logs the error and resets the listener flag. A failure leaves the old provider in place without cleanup, and the next request may open another connection. The full URL embeds the key, so any logging of it would leak the key. -->
The destination is fixed, which removes user steering. Reliability and secrecy gaps remain:

- On error the old provider stays in place without cleanup, and the next request may open another connection.
- An empty key is the fallback.
- The full URL embeds the key, so any logging of it would leak the key.



**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="outbound-url-fetch-restrictions"></a><a id="outbound-url-fetch-restrictions-profile-image-url"></a>
#### 6.10.2 Outbound URL fetch restrictions

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - the server fetches any requester-supplied URL with no scheme, host or private-address allowlist.

This control is not evidenced in the repository.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): The server fetches a requester-supplied imageUrl with no scheme, host, or private-address allowlist. Only a cookie-token login check precedes it. On fetch failure the raw URL is stored as profileImage. Inspected scope: routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts. -->
Only a cookie-token login check precedes the fetch:

- SSRF through the profile image URL (🟠 [F-045](#f-045)).
- The fetched body is stored unchecked (🟡 [F-081](#f-081)).
- On failure the raw URL is stored as the profile image.
- Redirect validation is a substring match (🟡 [F-064](#f-064)).

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

### 6.11 Operations Runtime and Supply Chain Controls

**Build path.** [Figure 1b](#figure-1b) shows the inputs, build systems and release artifacts that Juice Shop evidences.

Highlighted path of 🟡 [F-073](#f-073) — Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled: npm registry → GitHub Actions → docker.io/bkimminich/juice-shop. The path stops where the evidence ends.

**Attack entries:**

- **Manipulated dependency**
  - 🟡 [F-073](#f-073) — Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled
  - 🟡 [F-075](#f-075) — Lockfile generation disabled causing non-deterministic dependency
  - 🟡 [F-130](#f-130) — Dependency lifecycle scripts run in secret-bearing release
- **Manipulated CI input**
  - 🟡 [F-074](#f-074) — Third-party GitHub Actions referenced by mutable branch and tags
  - 🟡 [F-076](#f-076) — Container base images referenced by mutable tag without digest
- **Replaced release artifact**
  - 🟡 [F-072](#f-072) — Missing Container Image Signing

**Not attributable to a build step:**

- 🟡 [F-128](#f-128) — Container Runs as Root


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-028](#w-028), [W-029](#w-029), [W-048](#w-048)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Missing

**Controls covered:**

**Assessment:** Third-party actions are SHA-pinned in the inspected workflows, but others use mutable tags (🟡 [F-074](#f-074)). <a id="llm-integration-security"></a>
#### 6.12.2 LLM Integration Security

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - policy limits live only in the prompt, and the route has no authentication or rate limit.

`routes/chat.ts` passes chat history to an LLM that can call tools for order lookup, product reviews and coupon generation.

**Security assessment**

Model output and input are trusted too far:

- Client-supplied history reaches the model (🟠 [F-031](#f-031)).
- Stored reviews returned as tool results can steer the model (🟡 [F-087](#f-087)).
- Coupon discounts have no server-side cap (🟠 [F-058](#f-058), 🟡 [F-144](#f-144)).
- The system prompt holds a business rule that can be extracted (🟡 [F-101](#f-101)).
- Consumption is unbounded (🟠 [F-049](#f-049)).

**Relevant findings**

- None.

### 6.13 Defense-in-Depth Summary

<!-- §6.13 FORMAT — NEVER a table, at most 120 words, no file:line. Two short bullet lists: **What holds** (the individual positive controls that exist, strongest first, e.g. distroless runtime image, RS256 algorithm choice) and **Repair first** (the control-boundary repairs that would restore layered defense, each with its mitigation link [M-NNN](#m-nnn)). Do NOT emit a Markdown table — `| header |` lines under §6.13 are a contract violation. -->

**What holds**

- SHA-pinned GitHub Actions.
- `RS256` as the token signing algorithm.
- Rate limits on reset and 2FA.
- A fixed `wss://` RPC endpoint.
- CodeQL and SCA scanning in CI.

**Repair first**

- Parameterize login and search queries.
- Move signing keys out of source and pin the algorithm.
- Add server-side field allowlists and ownership checks.
- Authenticate socket connections.
- Replace unsalted `MD5` with a salted slow hash.

<a id="w-001"></a>
### W-001 — Application data crosses into executable code

🔴 Critical · implementation weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

An interpreter evaluates application values as code. The affected path mixes data processing with executable program construction instead of constraining callers to predefined operations.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Server-side code execution from stored username

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-050](#f-050) — B2B order lines evaluated as code in vm and notevil

**Affected components:** [C-04](#c-04)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — replace evaluation of application values with a data parser or a fixed set of operations whose arguments remain data
- **Tactical** — ● [M-168](#m-168) — Remove eval and pass username as Pug locals, ◕ [M-199](#m-199) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-050)

<a id="w-002"></a>
### W-002 — Database access relies on concatenated queries

🔴 Critical · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Database queries are assembled from application values instead of passing those values through an enforced parameterised data-access path. This leaves every call site responsible for preserving query structure.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Raw SQL string interpolation.** Several routes build queries by concatenating request values, bypassing the data access layer's parameter binding.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-003](#f-003) — SQL injection in product search query
- 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — SQL injection in login credential query allows

**Practice sites:**

- `routes/login.ts:34`
- `routes/search.ts:23`

**Architecture evidence:** Parameterized Queries, ORM / Repository Layer, Source evidence (`routes/vulnCodeSnippet.ts:46`)

**Affected components:** [C-04](#c-04), [C-06](#c-06)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — provide one parameterised repository or query-builder path and prohibit application-value interpolation in database queries
- **Tactical** — ● [M-166](#m-166) — Use parameterized replacements for search criteria, ● [M-167](#m-167) — Use parameterized queries for login lookup

<a id="w-003"></a>
### W-003 — Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

🟠 High · implementation weakness · confirmed · 6 findings

Token validation is implemented in application code rather than behind one vetted and consistently configured authentication path. Algorithm, issuer, audience, and key-handling checks can diverge between callers.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-012](#f-012) — Chatbot order lookup trusts identity from an unverified JWT
- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — Coupons accepted without integrity protection

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist
- 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — JWT verification without a pinned signature algorithm
- 🟡 [F-070](#f-070) — JWT verification accepts the algorithm from the token
- 🟡 [F-072](#f-072) — Missing Container Image Signing
- `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:68`

**Affected components:** [C-01](#c-01), [C-04](#c-04), [C-05](#c-05), [C-06](#c-06)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — centralise token validation on a vetted library with pinned algorithms, issuer and audience checks, and managed signing keys
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-169](#m-169) — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat identity, ◕ [M-177](#m-177) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims, ● [M-136](#m-136) — Pin allowed JWT algorithms on verification, ◑ [M-145](#m-145) — Manual review: verify JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist, ◕ [M-173](#m-173) — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call, ◕ [M-215](#m-215) — Pin RS256 in every token verification call, ◑ [M-140](#m-140) — Sign release container images and publish attestations, ◑ [M-152](#m-152) — Manual review: verify Missing Container Image Signing

<a id="w-004"></a>
### W-004 — Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

🟠 High · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Request handlers do not validate input against one enforced server-side schema or allowlist at the boundary - validation is either absent on the vulnerable parameters or limited to rejecting selected bad patterns (a blacklist). New encodings and unanticipated input forms can therefore reach downstream parsers or interpreters.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — Zip slip in file upload allows writes anywhere under

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — Request-chosen layout path allows file access

**Architecture evidence:** Schema Validation, Allowlist Validation, Source evidence (`server.ts:204`), Source evidence (`server.ts:246`), Source evidence (`frontend/src/app/photo-wall/photo-wall.component.ts:93`), Source evidence (`frontend/src/hacking-instructor/helpers/helpers.ts:262`) (+4 more)

**Affected components:** [C-04](#c-04)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce server-side schemas and domain-specific allowlists before input reaches parsing, persistence, or command construction
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-176](#m-176) — Confine zip entries to the extraction directory, ◑ [M-147](#m-147) — Manual review: verify Request-chosen layout path allows file access outside views…, ◕ [M-175](#m-175) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory (F-023)

🟠 High · design weakness · confirmed · 23 findings

Authorization depends on per-handler checks instead of a policy boundary that consistently enforces role, ownership, and tenant scope. New routes can bypass protection by omitting a local check.

- 🟡 [F-138](#f-138) — Any authenticated user can read any user record by id
- 🟡 [F-141](#f-141) — Deluxe membership granted without payment verification
- 🟡 [F-146](#f-146) — Missing role check on authenticated user

<a id="w-006"></a>
### W-006 — Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

🟠 High · implementation weakness · confirmed · 5 findings

Model binding accepts caller-selected attributes across a privileged write boundary. The affected binding path does not separate fields callers may change from fields reserved for server-side policy.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Mass-assignment / unscoped object binding.** Generated resources bind whole request bodies to models with no write allowlist, so privileged fields like role can be set by callers.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-061](#f-061) — Registration lets the request body set the admin role
- 🟡 [F-142](#f-142) — Client-supplied UserId accepted on feedback and complaint

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-059](#f-059) — User registration accepts a client-supplied admin role
- 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Mass assignment of role during user registration
- 🟡 [F-143](#f-143) — Security answer creation accepts owner UserId from request body
- `routes/basketItems.ts:70`

**Affected components:** [C-04](#c-04)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — define operation-specific write schemas and assign privileged attributes exclusively through server-side policy
- **Tactical** — ● [M-206](#m-206) — Allow-list registration fields and force the customer role, ◑ [M-282](#m-282) — Bind feedback UserId to the authenticated caller, ● [M-204](#m-204) — Strip role and privileged fields from registration input, ● [M-205](#m-205) — Allowlist registration fields and force customer role, ● [M-283](#m-283) — Bind security answers to the authenticated user

<a id="w-008"></a>
### W-008 — Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

🟠 High · design weakness · confirmed · 7 findings

Cryptographic keys, credentials, and other high-entropy secrets are embedded as literals in source or config rather than resolved at runtime from a managed secret store, so anyone with repository read access obtains reusable signing material and credentials.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Secrets hardcoded in source.** Token signing keys are literals in the repository, so anyone with the code can mint valid credentials.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — JWT signing private key is embedded in source code
- 🟡 [F-079](#f-079) — Forgeable continue codes

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Hardcoded JWT signing private key and HMAC keys in source
- 🟡 [F-111](#f-111) — Hardcoded cookie signing secret in server setup
- 🟡 [F-115](#f-115) — Hardcoded test account credentials in client bundle
- 🟡 [F-116](#f-116) — Hardcoded test account credentials in the client bundle
- 🟡 [F-121](#f-121) — Cleartext account passwords embedded in login handler
- `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`
- `frontend/src/hacking-instructor/challenges/loginBender.ts:84`
- `lib/insecurity.ts:42`
- `models/securityAnswer.ts:45`

**Architecture evidence:** Managed Secret Store, Runtime Secret Injection, Source evidence (`routes/checkKeys.ts:10`), Source evidence (`lib/insecurity.ts:21`)

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02), [C-04](#c-04), [C-05](#c-05), [C-06](#c-06), [C-08](#c-08)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — move every secret to a managed secret store or injected environment configuration, rotate the exposed values, and add secret-scanning to CI
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-172](#m-172) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (F-016), ◑ [M-221](#m-221) — Sign continue codes with a secret key, ● [M-171](#m-171) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (F-015), ◑ [M-253](#m-253) — Move cookie signing secret to external configuration, ◑ [M-157](#m-157) — Manual review: verify Hardcoded test account credentials in client bundle, ◑ [M-257](#m-257) — Remove embedded test credentials from frontend code, ◑ [M-258](#m-258) — Remove embedded test credentials from client code, ◑ [M-263](#m-263) — Remove embedded account passwords from source

<a id="w-009"></a>
### W-009 — Document-database queries take their structure from application values

🟠 High · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

Document-store queries take operators or JavaScript predicates from application values instead of fixed query structures with typed values. Callers can change what a query selects, for example through injected operators or evaluated expressions.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — NoSQL \$where JavaScript injection in product reviews and order tracking

**Affected components:** [C-04](#c-04)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — build document queries from fixed operator structures, coerce request values to the expected scalar types, and disable server-side JavaScript evaluation in the database
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-178](#m-178) — Use typed query predicates instead of \$where

<a id="w-010"></a>
### W-010 — Browser scripts retain reusable session credentials

🟠 High · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

Browser code retains reusable authentication credentials in script-readable storage. Script execution in the same origin can obtain the credentials and reuse the authenticated session.

**Architectural anti-pattern - SPA without BFF.** The browser app stores the session token where page scripts can read it, and no server-side session layer holds it. A single XSS flaw therefore yields reusable credentials.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — Session JWT stored in script-readable browser storage
- `frontend/src/app/chatbot/chat-conversation/chat-conversation.component.ts:107`

**Affected components:** [C-05](#c-05)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — move reusable credentials behind a server-managed session boundary and keep browser-visible state free of reusable authentication secrets
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-164](#m-164) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

<a id="w-011"></a>
### W-011 — Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

🟡 Medium · design weakness · confirmed · 3 findings

Sensitive API routes and real-time channels are exposed without an enforced authentication check at the endpoint boundary. Access control depends on each handler (or the caller) remembering to require a session, so an unauthenticated client can reach privileged operations directly.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟡 [F-066](#f-066) — Product reviews created anonymously
- 🟡 [F-091](#f-091) — Unauthenticated websocket events alter global
- 🟡 [F-093](#f-093) — Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Architecture evidence:** Route Authentication Middleware, Server-Side Session Enforcement, Source evidence (`server.ts:309`), Source evidence (`server.ts:402`), Source evidence (`server.ts:404`), Source evidence (`server.ts:406`) (+4 more)

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03), [C-04](#c-04), [C-06](#c-06)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce authentication centrally at the routing and channel boundary so every exposed endpoint requires a verified session unless explicitly marked public
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-211](#m-211) — Require authentication and derive review author server-side, ◑ [M-233](#m-233) — Authenticate socket connections before handling events, ◕ [M-235](#m-235) — Authenticate socket handshake and omit flags

### W-018 — Application data builds operating-system commands

🟡 Medium · implementation weakness · observed-practice

A command line is assembled from application values and handed to a shell. Shell syntax in those values runs as further commands with the privileges of the service.

**Practice sites:**

- `.github/workflows/update-news-www.yml:19`

**Affected components:** [C-01](#c-01)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — call a fixed executable with an argument list and no shell, and accept only allowlisted argument values from callers

<a id="w-019"></a>
### W-019 — Application data crosses into template source

🟡 Medium · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

Template construction treats application values as template source. Rendering can therefore interpret data as template instructions.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Stored username compiled into Pug template source

**Affected components:** [C-04](#c-04)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — compile fixed templates and pass application values only through their data-binding interface
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-179](#m-179) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-030)

<a id="w-026"></a>
### W-026 — OAuth sign-in requests are not bound to the user's browser session

🟡 Medium · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

The client starts OAuth or OpenID Connect sign-in without a per-request state value that its callback verifies. A forged authorization response can then complete sign-in in the victim's browser with the attacker's grant.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟡 [F-069](#f-069) — OAuth implicit flow without state, nonce or PKCE

**Affected components:** [C-05](#c-05)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — start every sign-in through a maintained OAuth/OIDC client that generates and verifies state (and PKCE) for each authorization request
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-214](#m-214) — Use authorization code flow with PKCE and state

<a id="w-027"></a>
### W-027 — Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

🟡 Medium · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 2 findings

A state-changing operation accepts ambient browser credentials without an enforced check that the authenticated user initiated the request. The affected mutation path lacks a request-authenticity boundary.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟡 [F-080](#f-080) — Cookie-authenticated profile update
- 🟡 [F-090](#f-090) — Chatbot auto-sends attacker-supplied
- `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:21`

**Affected components:** [C-04](#c-04), [C-05](#c-05)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce framework CSRF protection or validated origin and anti-CSRF tokens on every mutation that accepts ambient browser credentials
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-222](#m-222) — Require CSRF token and SameSite=Strict cookie, ◑ [M-232](#m-232) — Require explicit user confirmation before sending URL messages

- **Structural** — issue sensitive cookies server-side with HttpOnly protection and keep script-readable application preferences separate from session credentials

<a id="w-047"></a>
### W-047 — Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

🟡 Medium · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 3 findings

Password, token, or integrity protection uses a weak hash, predictable random source, or insufficient work factor. The application may use a standard library, but the selected primitive does not provide the required security property.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token
- 🟠 [F-048](#f-048) — Passwords stored as unsalted MD5 hashes
- 🟡 [F-112](#f-112) — Passwords verified and stored as unsalted fast

**Affected components:** [C-04](#c-04), [C-06](#c-06)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — standardise on a password KDF, a CSPRNG for secrets, and modern authenticated cryptographic primitives with centrally reviewed parameters
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-137](#m-137) — Use a CSPRNG for security tokens and secrets, ◑ [M-146](#m-146) — Manual review: verify Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token, ◕ [M-197](#m-197) — Hash passwords with bcrypt or argon2id, ◑ [M-254](#m-254) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm (F-112)

<a id="w-048"></a>
### W-048 — Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

🟡 Medium · design weakness · design-risk · 3 findings

CI/CD workflows resolve third-party actions and other build dependencies to mutable tags or branches instead of immutable commit digests, so a retagged or compromised upstream runs inside the pipeline with its token and secret scope.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟡 [F-074](#f-074) — Third-party GitHub Actions referenced by mutable branch and tags
- 🟡 [F-075](#f-075) — Lockfile generation disabled causing non-deterministic dependency — .npmrc:1
- 🟡 [F-130](#f-130) — Dependency lifecycle scripts run in secret-bearing release
- `Dockerfile:4`

**Architecture evidence:** Commit-SHA Action Pinning, Dependency Provenance Verification, Source evidence (`.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33`), Source evidence (`.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:42`), Source evidence (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:188`)

**Affected components:** [C-01](#c-01)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — pin every third-party action and build dependency to an immutable commit SHA (or a vetted internal mirror) and enforce SHA-pinning in CI
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-217](#m-217) — Pin image workflow actions to full commit SHAs, ◑ [M-218](#m-218) — Commit lockfiles and install from them, ◑ [M-160](#m-160) — Manual review: verify Dependency lifecycle scripts run in secret-bearing release…, ◑ [M-270](#m-270) — Isolate install scripts from privileged release steps

## 8. Findings Register

Findings are grouped by severity (Critical → High → Medium → Low); within a tier they are ordered by attack vektor (Repo-Read → Internet-Anon → Internet-User → Victim-Required). Every finding card shows **Severity · Component · Location**, **Issue**, **Fix**, and **Classification** (with external CWE / OWASP links). **Evidence**, **Root cause**, violated requirements, the weakness, and the trust boundary gap appear when they apply.

**Risk Distribution:** 🔴 Critical: 4 · 🟠 High: 46 · 🟡 Medium: 84 · 🟢 Low: n/a · **Total findings: 134**
*This register counts every finding card below. The Management Summary's risk distribution (Total: 72) counts folded insecure-practice sites under their weakness and adds each design risk once.*

The root causes and the fixes to do first are summarized in **Remediation Priorities** in the Management Summary; evidence-backed weaknesses are documented in the [Weakness Register](#weakness-register).

### 🔴 Critical (4)

<a id="t-003"></a><a id="f-003"></a>
#### F-003 · SQL injection in product search query

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-01](#tc-input-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Issue:** At `routes/search.ts:23`, the q parameter of GET `/rest/products/search` is interpolated into a raw SQL string in the LIKE clause and executed. Its only protection is truncation to 200 characters, with no escaping or binding.

The route has no authentication guard (`server.ts:602`). The scanner warns that request-controlled text in SQL can more generally let an attacker exfiltrate or modify arbitrary rows, bypass authentication or take full control of the database.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  20 │   return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  21 │     let criteria: any = req.query.q === 'undefined' ? '' : req.query.q ?? ''
  22 │     criteria = (criteria.length <= 200) ? criteria : criteria.substring(0, 200)
→ 23 │     models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Products WHERE ((name LIKE '%${criteria}%' OR description LIKE '%${criteria}%') AND deletedAt IS NULL) ORDER BY name`) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line unionSqlInjectionChallenge dbSchemaChallenge
  24 │       .then(([products]: any) => {
  25 │         const dataString = JSON.stringify(products)
  26 │         if (challengeUtils.notSolved(challenges.unionSqlInjectionChallenge)) { // vuln-code-snippet hide-start
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-166](#m-166) — Use parameterized replacements for search criteria

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-01](#tc-input-01) · Rules: INJ-001 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.3](#33-sql-injection-in-product-search-query)

<a id="t-005"></a><a id="f-005"></a>
#### F-005 · Broken authorization

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/dataExport.ts:26`

**Contradicted by verification:** a control was evidenced at [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) at `routes/dataExport.ts:26`. Check this finding before acting on it.

**Reliability:** Low. This may be a false positive: a review or check contradicts it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A signed-in attacker puts another user's ID into the owner field of the request (`req.body.UserId` and the like); the query trusts it instead of the session identity, so the attacker reads or changes every other user's records - horizontal privilege escalation across all accounts.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  23 │
  24 │         let memories, orders, reviews
  25 │         try {
→ 26 │           memories = await MemoryModel.findAll({ where: { UserId: req.body.UserId } })
  27 │         } catch (error) {
  28 │           next(error)
  29 │           return
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-135](#m-135) — Derive record owner from the session, not the request body · ● [M-144](#m-144) — Manual review: verify Broken authorization attacker-controlled owner ID in resource query…

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) · Rules: AUTHZ-001 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.1](#31-broken-authorization-in-data-export)

<a id="t-010"></a><a id="f-010"></a>
#### F-010 · Server-side code execution from stored username

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Application data crosses into executable code

**Issue:** An authenticated user sets their username through POST `/profile` to #{`global.process.mainModule.require('child_process').execSync('id').toString()`}; `updateUserProfile` stores it unmodified. The persisted record is read, and on GET `/profile` the handler matches #{...} and passes the inner code to `eval()`, so it runs in the Node process with the server's privileges and the command output is rendered back.

The username is also spliced into Pug source that `pug.compile` compiles, a second path to running attacker JavaScript. One part notes the username write path was not read, and the scanner part leaves attacker control and active configuration for verification.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  58 │         if (!code) {
  59 │           throw new Error('Username is null')
  60 │         }
→ 61 │         username = eval(code) // eslint-disable-line no-eval
  62 │       } catch (err) {
  63 │         username = '\\' + username
  64 │       }
```

**Fix:** Replace runtime code generation (eval/Function/template render) with a data-only execution path → ● [M-168](#m-168) — Remove eval and pass username as Pug locals

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03) · Rules: INJ-NODE-007 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.4](#34-server-side-code-execution-from-stored-username-in-user-profile)

<a id="t-009"></a><a id="f-009"></a>
#### F-009 · SQL injection in login credential query allows

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-01](#tc-input-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-4](#tb-4) 🌐 External - external → Authentication &amp; Session Surface · Validation: The validation leg assumes the login email reaches the Users query only as a bound value. Here it is concatenated into the SQL text.

**Issue:** At `routes/login.ts:34`, the login handler interpolates `req.body.email` from a request into a raw SQL string. An unauthenticated attacker sends POST `/rest/user/login` with an email such as admin@juice-`sh.op`'-- and any password.

The quote closes the email literal, and the comment drops the password and `deletedAt` conditions. The TOTP gate applies only to accounts that have a secret set, and it runs only after the injectable query returns a row.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  31 │
  32 │   return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  33 │     verifyPreLoginChallenges(req) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
→ 34 │     models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email || ''}' AND password = '${security.hash(req.body.password || '')}' AND deletedAt IS NULL`, { model: UserModel, plain: true }) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
  35 │       .then((authenticatedUser) => { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
  36 │         const user = utils.queryResultToJson(authenticatedUser)
  37 │         if (user.data?.id && user.data.totpSecret !== '') {
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-167](#m-167) — Use parameterized queries for login lookup

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-01](#tc-input-01) · Rules: INJ-001 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.2](#32-sql-injection-in-login-credential-query-allows-authentication-bypass)

### 🟠 High (46)

<a id="t-015"></a><a id="f-015"></a>
#### F-015 · Hardcoded JWT signing private key and HMAC keys in source

**Severity:** 🟠 High - secret committed to the public source repo - extractable on clone, no prior access needed; elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-005 (Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** The `RS256` private key that `authorize()` uses to sign every session token is a literal, and it is never rotated. The public key is read from a file (line 20), but the private key has no external store.

With the HMAC keys, the same person can compute deluxe tokens and precompute security-answer HMACs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A signing key is embedded as a literal constant in source.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ● [M-171](#m-171) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (🟠 [F-015](#f-015))

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.8](#38-hardcoded-jwt-signing-private-key-and-hmac-keys-in-source)

<a id="t-016"></a><a id="f-016"></a>
#### F-016 · JWT signing private key is embedded in source code

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:54`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** The `RS256` private key is a literal in `lib/insecurity.ts`. An attacker who obtains the source, for example from the public repository, signs a token with any user and role data. `isAuthorized` and `security.verify` accept it because it verifies against the matching public key.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `authorize()` signs with `privateKey`, which line 21 defines as an embedded PEM literal. The verifiers at lines 52 and 55 trust any token that key signs.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ◕ [M-172](#m-172) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (🟠 [F-016](#f-016))

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02)

<a id="t-001"></a><a id="f-001"></a>
#### F-001 · Session JWT stored in script-readable browser storage

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Browser scripts retain reusable session credentials

**Instances (3):** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts`: 101; `frontend/src/app/payment/payment.component.ts`: 232; `frontend/src/app/two-factor-auth-enter/two-factor-auth-enter.component.ts`: 53

**Issue:** After any login, the client writes the bearer JWT to `localStorage` and to a cookie set from JavaScript. Verification lists the password, OAuth, 2FA and deluxe-upgrade logins. The finding holds 3 places in total, more than the location cited here.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:101` runs `localStorage.setItem('token', ...)` and line 104 runs `cookieService.put('token', ...)` with only an expiry. `oauth.component.ts:50-51` does the same. `request.interceptor.ts:16` reads the token from `localStorage` for the `Authorization` header.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-164](#m-164) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02)

<a id="t-002"></a><a id="f-002"></a>
#### F-002 · Missing ownership checks on basket endpoints allow cross-user basket access

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-B-007 (User misuses a privileged function for another purpose); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (8)

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-3](#tb-3) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express Server · Authorization: The basket id from the path is not checked against the authenticated user, which breaks the authorization leg of the JWT ingress boundary.

**Instances (8):** `routes/basket.ts`: 19; `routes/basketItems.ts`: 68; `routes/coupon.ts`: 18; `routes/order.ts`: 35; `server.ts`: 426, 603, 604, 605

**Issue:** Several basket endpoints load a basket or basket item by an id from the request. They require authentication through `isAuthorized`, and `appendUserId` on `/rest/basket` (`server.ts:356`, 399), but never check that the object belongs to the caller.

The finding holds 8 locations, more than the locations its parts cite.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
server.ts
  424 │   app.use('/b2b/v2', security.isAuthorized())
  425 │   /* Check if the quantity is available in stock and limit per user not exceeded, then add item to basket */
→ 426 │   app.put('/api/BasketItems/:id', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(basketItems.quantityCheckBeforeBasketItemUpdate()))
  427 │   app.post('/api/BasketItems', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(basketItems.quantityCheckBeforeBasketItemAddition()), utils.asyncHandler(basketItems.addBasketItem()))
  428 │   /* Accounting users are allowed to check and update quantities */
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-165](#m-165) — Scope basket lookup to the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) · Rules: AUTHZ-301

<a id="t-012"></a><a id="f-012"></a>
#### F-012 · Chatbot order lookup trusts identity from an unverified JWT

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:45`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AI-04](#tc-ai-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** At `routes/chat.ts:45`, `getUserId` decodes the bearer token with `security.decode` (`jws.decode`, `insecurity.ts:56`) and never verifies its signature. An attacker sends POST `/rest/chat` with a self-made or unsigned JWT whose `data.id` is the victim's user id and asks for one of the victim's orders.

In the survey's terms (ASI03), the tool acts on an identity the agent never verified.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  43 │   const token = utils.jwtFrom(req)
  44 │   if (!token) return undefined
→ 45 │   const decoded = security.decode(token) as { data?: { id?: number } } | undefined
  46 │   return decoded?.data?.id
  47 │ }
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◕ [M-169](#m-169) — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat identity

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AI-04](#tc-ai-04)

<a id="t-013"></a><a id="f-013"></a>
#### F-013 · JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-003 (Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT Algorithm Confusion); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases) _(raw Critical)_  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: verify call lacks an algorithms option, but it only feeds challenge-solving, not an auth gate; forgery impact is not shown in this window.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Instances (2):** `routes/verify.ts`: 120; `lib/insecurity.ts`: 189

**Issue:** Without an explicit algorithm allowlist, attackers can forge tokens with `alg:none` (older lib versions) or use the public key as an HMAC secret to mint valid signatures.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
routes/verify.ts
  118 │     }
  119 │
→ 120 │     jwt.verify(token, security.publicKey, (err: jwt.VerifyErrors | null) => {
  121 │       if (err === null) {
  122 │         challengeUtils.solveIf(challenge, () => {
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ● [M-136](#m-136) — Pin allowed JWT algorithms on verification · ◑ [M-145](#m-145) — Manual review: verify JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02) · Rules: AUTHZ-005 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.5](#35-jwt-verify-call-missing-algorithms-allowlist-algorithm-confusion)

<a id="t-014"></a><a id="f-014"></a>
#### F-014 · OAuth account password predictably derived

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-10](#tb-10) - Angular Frontend → external · Response trust: The browser takes the email from Google's userinfo response and turns it into a local credential without server-side token verification, which breaks the response-trust assumption.

**Issue:** After a Google sign-in, the client registers and logs in the local account with the password `btoa(reversed email)`. The derivation is deterministic and ships in the public bundle. The login request sends an `oauth: true` flag, but the survey did not read any server-side handling of it.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 30 sets password = `btoa(reversed email)` and line 31 registers it via POST `/api/Users`. Line 46 logs in with the same derived password through `/rest/user/login`. The server login handler that verifies it was not read.

```typescript
  28 │     this.userService.oauthLogin(this.parseRedirectUrlParams().access_token).subscribe({
  29 │       next: (profile: any) => {
→ 30 │         const password = btoa(profile.email.split('').reverse().join(''))
  31 │         this.userService.save({ email: profile.email, password, passwordRepeat: password }).subscribe({
  32 │           next: () => {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-170](#m-170) — Exchange OAuth tokens server-side instead of derived passwords

**Classification:** OAuth / OIDC Misconfiguration · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07)

#### F-022 · Password reset by security answer with spoofable

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-B-003 (User takes over another account); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:41`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker posts the victim's email and guessed security answers to `/rest/user/reset-password`. The limiter keys on X-Forwarded-For or on `req.ip`, which trust proxy derives from that header.

Rotating the header defeats the 100 requests per 5 minutes cap, and a correct guess sets the attacker's new password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Recovery succeeds when `hmac(answer)` equals the stored answer, then the password is updated at line 44. The rate limit at `server.ts:342-346` is keyed on a client-controlled IP.

```typescript
  39 │         }]
  40 │       })
→ 41 │       if ((data != null) && security.hmac(answer) === data.answer) {
  42 │         const user = await UserModel.findByPk(data.UserId)
  43 │         if (user) {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-174](#m-174) — Replace answer-based reset with emailed one-time tokens

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05)

#### F-024 · Zip slip in file upload allows writes anywhere under

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:34`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01), [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

**Issue:** At `routes/fileUpload.ts:34`, an unauthenticated attacker uploads a .zip to `/file-upload` with entries named, for example, ../..`/ftp/legal.md` or ../..`/frontend/dist/frontend/main.js`. Each entry path is joined to uploads/complaints/.

The verification part notes that this behavior is gated by the `fileWriteChallenge` flag.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript

  33 │     if (absolutePath.includes(path.resolve('.'))) {
→ 34 │       await pipeline(entry.stream(), fs.createWriteStream('uploads/complaints/' + fileName))
  35 │     }
  36 │   }
```

**Fix:** Resolve and normalise every constructed path and reject anything that escapes the intended base directory → ◕ [M-176](#m-176) — Confine zip entries to the extraction directory

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01), [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02)

<a id="t-025"></a><a id="f-025"></a>
#### F-025 · Coupons accepted without integrity protection

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/coupon.ts:15`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** `applyCoupon` accepts whatever `security.discountFromCoupon` decodes. That function is a `z85` decode of 'MMMYY-NN' checked only by format and current month, with no MAC or server-side record.

An authenticated attacker can z85-encode a string such as 'NOV26-99' and apply a 99% discount to their basket.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  13 │       const id = params.id
  14 │       let coupon: string | undefined | null = params.coupon ? decodeURIComponent(params.coupon) : undefined
→ 15 │       const discount = security.discountFromCoupon(coupon)
  16 │       coupon = discount ? coupon : null
  17 │
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-177](#m-177) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

#### F-026 · IDOR

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-B-001 (User reads or changes data of another user or tenant); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:68`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Line 68 only loads the item for a quantity check; the actual update in the finale handler was not served, so unscoped modification is not shown.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** Any authenticated user can substitute another user's resource ID in the URL and read or modify the target record because the query filter has no ownership clause. Admin-only routes are excluded via the counter-pattern allowlist.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  66 │   return async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  67 │     try {
→ 68 │       const item = await BasketItemModel.findOne({ where: { id: req.params.id } })
  69 │       const user = security.authenticatedUsers.from(req)
  70 │       challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.basketManipulateChallenge, () => { return user && req.body.BasketId && user.bid != req.body.BasketId }) // eslint-disable-line eqeqeq
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-139](#m-139) — Scope basket item lookups to the caller · ◑ [M-148](#m-148) — Manual review: verify IDOR resource lookup by URL parameter without ownership filter…

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) · Rules: AUTHZ-002

<a id="t-029"></a><a id="f-029"></a>
#### F-029 · NoSQL \$where JavaScript injection in product reviews and order tracking

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Document-database queries take their structure from application values

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-8](#tb-8) - Juice Shop Express Server → MarsDB Store · Query construction: The path id is interpreted as JavaScript in a \$where predicate instead of as a literal value.

**Instances (2):** `routes/showProductReviews.ts`: 36; `routes/trackOrder.ts`: 18

**Issue:** At `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`, when `noSqlCommandChallenge` is enabled, the product id path segment read at line 31 is only truncated to 40 characters and concatenated into a MarsDB \$where JavaScript predicate; `Number()` coercion applies only when the challenge is disabled. An unauthenticated attacker requests `/rest/products/``<payload>``/reviews`, for example `sleep(2000)` to block the event loop on each request (`global.sleep` is capped at 2000ms per call) or 1||true to match and read every review, and can run arbitrary predicate code.

The scanner parts leave attacker control and active configuration for verification.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
routes/showProductReviews.ts
  34 │     const t0 = new Date().getTime()
  35 │
→ 36 │     db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + id }).then((reviews: Review[]) => {
  37 │       const t1 = new Date().getTime()
  38 │       challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.noSqlCommandChallenge, () => { return (t1 - t0) > 2000 })
```

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◕ [M-178](#m-178) — Use typed query predicates instead of \$where

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02) · Rules: INJ-NODE-006

<a id="t-040"></a><a id="f-040"></a>
#### F-040 · Unauthenticated memories endpoint returns full user

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/memory.ts:24`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker sends GET `/rest/memories`. The handler runs `MemoryModel.findAll` with include [UserModel] and no attribute restriction, so the response holds every column of each uploader's User record: email, the unsalted `MD5` password hash, role, `deluxeToken` and `totpSecret`.

The attacker cracks the hashes and uses the TOTP secrets to take over accounts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  22 │ export function getMemories () {
  23 │   return async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
→ 24 │     const memories = await MemoryModel.findAll({ include: [UserModel] })
  25 │     res.status(200).json({ status: 'success', data: memories })
  26 │   }
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-189](#m-189) — Restrict included user attributes in memories response

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker calls GET `/rest/memories`. The route has no auth middleware, and the handler runs `findAll` with include: [UserModel] and no attribute restriction. The response carries each owner's email, `MD5` password hash, role, `deluxeToken` and `totpSecret`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript

→ 24 │     const memories = await MemoryModel.findAll({ include: [UserModel] })
  25 │     res.status(200).json({ status: 'success', data: memories })
  26 │   }
```
