**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-359](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/359.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-042"></a><a id="f-042"></a>
#### F-042 · XML external entity resolution on unauthenticated upload

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `lib/xml.ts:35`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Handler parses XML with XXE options and echoes 400 chars, but the `/file-upload` route registration and its auth were not served, so 'unauthenticated' is unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-022](#w-022) - XML external entity resolution on unauthenticated upload (`xml.ts:35`)

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads an .xml file to `/file-upload` whose DTD defines an external entity for `file:///etc/passwd`. `parseXmlString` parses with NOENT and DTDLOAD and has filesystem input providers registered, so the entity resolves.

The handler echoes up to 400 characters of the expanded document in its error.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  33 │ export async function parseXmlString (data: string, timeoutMs = 2000): Promise<string> {
  34 │   const libxml2 = await loadLibxml2()
→ 35 │   const option = libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOENT | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_DTDLOAD | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOBLANKS | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOCDATA
  36 │   const sandbox = { libxml2, data, option }
  37 │   vm.createContext(sandbox)
```

**Fix:** Disable external entity resolution on every XML parser and reject DOCTYPE declarations → ◑ [M-150](#m-150) — Manual review: verify XML external entity resolution on unauthenticated upload · ◕ [M-191](#m-191) — Disable entity substitution and DTD loading

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-043"></a><a id="f-043"></a>
#### F-043 · XML parser enables external entity and DTD loading

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `lib/xml.ts:35`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-023](#w-023) - XML parser enables external entity and DTD loading (`xml.ts:35`)

**Issue:** `parseXmlString` parses with `XML_PARSE_NOENT` and `XML_PARSE_DTDLOAD` after registering host filesystem input providers. Any XML an attacker submits to a caller can therefore resolve external entities such as `file:///etc/passwd` and return the expanded content.

The calling upload route was not in this job's reads, so reachability is not shown here.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  33 │ export async function parseXmlString (data: string, timeoutMs = 2000): Promise<string> {
  34 │   const libxml2 = await loadLibxml2()
→ 35 │   const option = libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOENT | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_DTDLOAD | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOBLANKS | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOCDATA
  36 │   const sandbox = { libxml2, data, option }
  37 │   vm.createContext(sandbox)
```

**Fix:** Disable external entity resolution on every XML parser and reject DOCTYPE declarations → ◕ [M-192](#m-192) — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution (🟠 [F-043](#f-043))

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-044"></a><a id="f-044"></a>
#### F-044 · XML parser resolves external entities from host filesystem

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `lib/xml.ts:38`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-06](#tc-input-06))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads an .xml file to `/file-upload` that declares an external entity such as `file:///etc/passwd`. libxml2 is called with NOENT and DTDLOAD and has host filesystem providers registered, so it substitutes the file contents. The parsed text is put into the error message.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  36 │   const sandbox = { libxml2, data, option }
  37 │   vm.createContext(sandbox)
→ 38 │   const xmlDoc = vm.runInContext('libxml2.XmlDocument.fromString(data, { option })', sandbox, { timeout: timeoutMs })
  39 │   const xmlString = xmlDoc.toString()
  40 │   xmlDoc.dispose()
```

**Fix:** Disable external entity resolution on every XML parser and reject DOCTYPE declarations → ◕ [M-193](#m-193) — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution (🟠 [F-044](#f-044))

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-06](#tc-input-06)

<a id="t-045"></a><a id="f-045"></a>
#### F-045 · Server-side request forgery

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-05](#tc-input-05))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-11](#tb-11) - Juice Shop Express Server → external · Egress destination: `imageUrl` reaches `fetch(url)` without restricting the destination to permitted public hosts.

**Issue:** At `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`, a logged-in user posts an `imageUrl` to `/profile/image/url` that points to an internal address, such as `http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/` (cloud metadata), localhost admin ports or another internal service. The server fetches the URL without checking the scheme, host or private address, and it follows redirects by default.

The only control before the fetch is the cookie-token session lookup (lines 21-22).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  22 │       if (loggedInUser) {
  23 │         try {
→ 24 │           const response = await fetch(url)
  25 │           if (!response.ok || !response.body) {
  26 │             throw new Error('url returned a non-OK status code or an empty body')
```

**Fix:** Validate the URL scheme + host against an explicit allow-list before issuing outbound requests → ◕ [M-194](#m-194) — Validate and allowlist outbound request targets

**Classification:** Server-Side Request Forgery · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-05](#tc-input-05)

<a id="t-046"></a><a id="f-046"></a>
#### F-046 · Password reset response leaks password hash

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:46`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-031](#w-031) - Password reset response leaks password hash and TOTP secret

**Issue:** An attacker who guesses or researches a victim's security answer posts it to `/rest/user/reset-password`. The response holds the victim's full User record, including `totpSecret`. The attacker can generate valid TOTP codes and pass the second factor with the new password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Sensitive runtime values are served unauthenticated to any caller.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◕ [M-195](#m-195) — Return a minimal status from password reset

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-047"></a><a id="f-047"></a>
#### F-047 · Password reset response leaks hash and TOTP secret

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:46`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-6](#tb-6) 🌐 External - external → Authentication &amp; Session Surface · Authentication: The reset relies on the security answer alone and returns the full user row, so a known answer gives the attacker the account's 2FA secret as well.

**Issue:** After a successful security-answer check, `resetPassword` returns the whole updated user object, including the password hash and `totpSecret`. An attacker who guesses or researches a victim's security answer resets the password and receives the TOTP secret, so they can also defeat the victim's second factor at login.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  44 │           const updatedUser = await user.update({ password: newPassword })
  45 │           verifySecurityAnswerChallenges(updatedUser, answer)
→ 46 │           res.json({ user: updatedUser })
  47 │         }
  48 │       } else {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-196](#m-196) — Return only a status from password reset

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-201](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/201.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-049"></a><a id="f-049"></a>
#### F-049 · Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:203`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-016](#w-016) - Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint (`chat.ts:203`)

**Issue:** LLM10 - `/rest/chat` has no auth or rate limit and `streamText` sets no `maxOutputTokens` or message size bound; an attacker scripts large concurrent requests to exhaust provider quota and server resources. Only `stepCountIs(10)` and `maxRetries` bound work.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  201 │
  202 │     try {
→ 203 │       const result = streamText({
  204 │         model: provider(model),
  205 │         system: systemPrompt,
```

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◕ [M-198](#m-198) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (🟠 [F-049](#f-049))

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-050"></a><a id="f-050"></a>
#### F-050 · B2B order lines evaluated as code in vm and notevil

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Application data crosses into executable code

**Issue:** At `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`, POST `/b2b/v2/orders` passes `body.orderLinesData` to notevil `safeEval` inside a node:vm context. node:vm is not a security boundary, so any escape from the notevil interpreter executes code in the server process.

One part states the route requires only a valid JWT via `isAuthorized` on `/b2b/v2` (`server.ts:424`) and that registration is open; the other part notes the route guard at `server.ts:648` was not read.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  21 │         const sandbox = { safeEval, orderLinesData }
  22 │         vm.createContext(sandbox)
→ 23 │         vm.runInContext('safeEval(orderLinesData)', sandbox, { timeout: 2000 })
  24 │         res.json({ cid: body.cid, orderNo: uniqueOrderNumber(), paymentDue: dateTwoWeeksFromNow() })
  25 │       } catch (err) {
```

**Fix:** Replace runtime code generation (eval/Function/template render) with a data-only execution path → ◕ [M-199](#m-199) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (🟠 [F-050](#f-050))

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03)

<a id="t-051"></a><a id="f-051"></a>
#### F-051 · Password change skips current password check

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-014](#w-014) - Password change skips current password check when omitted (`changePassword.ts:39`)

**Issue:** At `routes/changePassword.ts:39`, the `changePassword` handler checks the current password only when `query.current` is supplied. An attacker who holds a victim's bearer token, for example stolen through stored XSS that reads `localStorage`, sends GET `/rest/user/change-password?new=Pwn3d`!&`repeat=Pwn3d`!

The only controls are that the token must be present in `authenticatedUsers` (`changePassword.ts:33`) and that the new and repeated passwords must match.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  37 │     }
  38 │
→ 39 │     if (currentPassword && security.hash(currentPassword) !== loggedInUser.data.password) {
  40 │       res.status(401).send(res.__('Current password is not correct.'))
  41 │       return
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-200](#m-200) — Require and verify current password on change

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05)

<a id="t-057"></a><a id="f-057"></a>
#### F-057 · Unauthenticated product update

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:501`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker sends PUT `/api/Products/:id` with a JSON body that sets price, name, description or image. No middleware runs before the finale-generated update handler, because the `isAuthorized` guard for this route is commented out.

The attacker can rewrite the catalog, for example by setting a price to 0.01 or injecting content into product descriptions.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts` registers Product with finale (lines 485, 501-503), and its PUT guard at line 370 is commented out. `Product.price` is a writable DECIMAL with no setter restriction (`models/product.ts` line 58).

```typescript
  499 │
  500 │   for (const { name, exclude, model, include } of autoModels) {
→ 501 │     const resource = finale.resource({
  502 │       model,
  503 │       endpoints: [`/api/${name}s`, `/api/${name}s/:id`],
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-202](#m-202) — Restrict product updates to administrators

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-058"></a><a id="f-058"></a>
#### F-058 · LLM coupon tool lacks server-side discount

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** LLM06/ASI02 - the 10% cap and verified-order requirement exist only in the system prompt. The `generateCoupon` tool schema accepts any number and `execute()` calls `security.generateCoupon(discount)` directly, so an anonymous caller who persuades the model (LLM01/ASI01) obtains coupons of arbitrary discount.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript

  183 │           challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotGreedyInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 50) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
→ 184 │           const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
  185 │           return { couponCode, discount } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
  186 │         }
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-203](#m-203) — Enforce coupon cap and eligibility in tool execute

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-059"></a><a id="f-059"></a>
#### F-059 · User registration accepts a client-supplied admin role

**Severity:** 🟠 High - reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint; elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-B-002 (User gains more rights than intended); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:408`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express Server · Validation: The finding's evidence line is the interface this boundary cites; CWE-915 breaks its validation condition.

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker sends POST `/api/Users` with {email, password, `passwordRepeat`, role:'admin'}. The pre-handler only trims fields.

The finale-generated User resource then creates the record from the whole body, and the model accepts 'admin' as a valid role, so the attacker gets an administrator account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:408-422` contain no role filtering. `server.ts:484` excludes only password and `totpSecret`, and only from output. `models/user.ts:83-97` accept and set the admin role. `verify.ts:53` confirms that a body role of admin reaches the create path. `Server.ts` lines 356-391 were not served, so the absence of a guard there is not shown.

```typescript
  406 │   app.post('/api/Feedbacks', verify.captchaBypassChallenge())
  407 │   /* User registration challenge verifications before finale takes over */
→ 408 │   app.post('/api/Users', (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  409 │     if (req.body.email !== undefined && req.body.password !== undefined && req.body.passwordRepeat !== undefined) {
  410 │       if (req.body.email.length !== 0 && req.body.password.length !== 0) {
```

**Fix:** ● [M-204](#m-204) — Strip role and privileged fields from registration input

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.6](#36-user-registration-accepts-a-client-supplied-admin-role)

<a id="t-060"></a><a id="f-060"></a>
#### F-060 · Mass assignment of role during user registration

**Severity:** 🟠 High - reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint; elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-004 (Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:484`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker sends POST `/api/Users` with email, password, `passwordRepeat` and role set to admin. The middlewares that run before finale (`server.ts` lines 408-422) only trim the email and password fields and record challenges.

In that case the attacker gets an administrator account and admin role claims in the JWT at login, which reach admin-only features.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  482 │
  483 │   const autoModels = [
→ 484 │     { name: 'User', exclude: ['password', 'totpSecret'], model: UserModel },
  485 │     { name: 'Product', exclude: [], model: ProductModel },
  486 │     { name: 'Feedback', exclude: [], model: FeedbackModel },
```

**Fix:** ● [M-205](#m-205) — Allowlist registration fields and force customer role

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.7](#37-mass-assignment-of-role-during-user-registration-in-juice-shop-express-server)

<a id="t-061"></a><a id="f-061"></a>
#### F-061 · Registration lets the request body set the admin role

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical: reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:501`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker sends POST `/api/Users` with `{"email":..., "password":..., "role":"admin"}`. The finale-generated create endpoint binds the whole body to the User model, and the account is created with the admin role.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts` line 484 sets User's exclude list to password and `totpSecret`, and line 501 creates the finale resource. `models/user.ts` line 83 allows admin, and line 97 sets the role.

```typescript
  499 │
  500 │   for (const { name, exclude, model, include } of autoModels) {
→ 501 │     const resource = finale.resource({
  502 │       model,
  503 │       endpoints: [`/api/${name}s`, `/api/${name}s/:id`],
```

**Fix:** ● [M-206](#m-206) — Allow-list registration fields and force the customer role

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-062"></a><a id="f-062"></a>
#### F-062 · Address update by id lacks owner check

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:450`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated customer sends PUT `/api/Addresss/`&lt;`victimAddressId`&gt; with any address fields. `appendUserId` only replaces `body.UserId` with the attacker's id, and the finale-generated update then loads the address by :id with no owner comparison.

The attacker overwrites another customer's delivery address and reassigns it to their own account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  448 │   app.post('/api/Addresss', security.appendUserId())
  449 │   app.get('/api/Addresss', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(address.getAddress()))
→ 450 │   app.put('/api/Addresss/:id', security.appendUserId())
  451 │   app.delete('/api/Addresss/:id', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(address.delAddressById()))
  452 │   app.get('/api/Addresss/:id', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(address.getAddressById()))
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-207](#m-207) — Scope address updates to the caller's UserId

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

**Issue:** Any registered user calls GET `/rest/user/authentication-details`. The only guard is `isAuthorized`, which checks the JWT signature. This user directory is meant for administrators.

```typescript
→ 601 │   app.get('/rest/user/authentication-details', utils.asyncHandler(authenticatedUsers()))
```

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-017"></a><a id="f-017"></a>
#### F-017 · Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:53`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: `Math.random()` seeds the `denyAll` secret, which is meant to reject everything. No session, reset or OTP token is generated here, and secret guessability is unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-047](#w-047) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** A predictable token/secret lets an attacker guess or brute-force session identifiers, reset links, or OTPs.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  51 │
  52 │ export const isAuthorized = () => expressJwt(({ secret: publicKey }) as any)
→ 53 │ export const denyAll = () => expressJwt({ secret: '' + Math.random() } as any)
  54 │ export const authorize = (user = {}) => jwt.sign(user, privateKey, { expiresIn: '6h', algorithm: 'RS256' })
  55 │ export const verify = (token: string) => token ? (jws.verify as ((token: string, secret: string) => boolean))(token, publicKey) : false
```

**Fix:** Switch to a cryptographically secure RNG (`crypto.randomBytes` / OS `/dev/urandom`) → ◕ [M-137](#m-137) — Use a CSPRNG for security tokens and secrets · ◑ [M-146](#m-146) — Manual review: verify Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-330](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/330.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-CRYPTO-02](#tc-crypto-02) · Rules: CRYPTO-002

<a id="t-020"></a><a id="f-020"></a>
#### F-020 · JWT verification without a pinned signature algorithm

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** `verify()` calls `jws.verify(token, publicKey)` and `isAuthorized()` configures `express-jwt` with only the public key; neither restricts the accepted algorithms. With library versions that honor the header alg, an attacker can submit an unsigned token or an `HS256` token keyed with the public key and claim role accounting/admin, passing `isAccounting`, `isDeluxe` and the 2FA `tmpToken` check.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  53 │ export const denyAll = () => expressJwt({ secret: '' + Math.random() } as any)
  54 │ export const authorize = (user = {}) => jwt.sign(user, privateKey, { expiresIn: '6h', algorithm: 'RS256' })
→ 55 │ export const verify = (token: string) => token ? (jws.verify as ((token: string, secret: string) => boolean))(token, publicKey) : false
  56 │ export const decode = (token: string) => { return jws.decode(token)?.payload }
  57 │
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◕ [M-173](#m-173) — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-048"></a><a id="f-048"></a>
#### F-048 · Passwords stored as unsalted MD5 hashes

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04), [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-047](#w-047) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Instances (2):** `lib/insecurity.ts`: 41; `models/user.ts`: 76

**Issue:** `hash()` uses unsalted `MD5`, which is fast and collision-broken, with no salt, pepper or work factor. The User model password setter at `models/user.ts:76` stores `security.hash(clearTextPassword)`, and login compares against these digests.

The scanner adds that `MD5` or `SHA-1` used for the integrity of attacker-influenced data can be forged.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A non-iterating hash is used for password storage.

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-197](#m-197) — Hash passwords with bcrypt or argon2id

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04), [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02), [TC-CRYPTO-01](#tc-crypto-01) · Rules: CRYPTO-001

<a id="t-032"></a><a id="f-032"></a>
#### F-032 · Stored XSS from feedback comments on the About page

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** At `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`, an attacker submits feedback whose comment contains HTML or script. The About page embeds the stored comment in an HTML template string and marks it trusted with `bypassSecurityTrustHtml`, so Angular skips sanitization.

Neither part examined server-side sanitization of stored comments.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `about.component.ts` lines 116-121 put feedbacks[i].comment into an HTML string and mark it trusted with DomSanitizer. `about.component.html` line 51 binds it via [`innerHTML`]. The comment arrives through POST `/api/Feedbacks`. The server's write path was not read, so any server-side sanitization is unknown.

```typescript
  117 │             feedbacks[i].comment
  118 │           }</p><div class="feedback-stars">(${this.stars[feedbacks[i].rating]})</div></figcaption>`
→ 119 │           feedbacks[i].comment = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(
  120 │             feedbacks[i].comment
  121 │           )
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-181](#m-181) — Stop trusting feedback HTML and use text bindings

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-035"></a><a id="f-035"></a>
#### F-035 · Stored XSS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Instances (2):** `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts`: 91; `frontend/src/app/feedback-details/feedback-details.component.html`: 18

**Issue:** At `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:91`, an attacker submits customer feedback, or registers a user email, that contains HTML or JavaScript. When an administrator opens the administration page, `findAllFeedbacks` marks `feedback.comment` as trusted with `bypassSecurityTrustHtml`, and the template renders it via [`innerHTML`].

The verification part says the comment is rendered the same way in the feedback details dialog. Script execution in an administrator session can exfiltrate the admin token and perform admin actions; in this training deployment no real business data is at risk.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `administration.component.ts:91` trusts `feedback.comment` as HTML and :73 interpolates `user.email` into a trusted HTML string; `administration.component.html:60` and :26 render them via [`innerHTML`]. The server-side feedback/user write path was not read, so persistence without sanitization is not shown.

```typescript
frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts
  89 │         this.feedbackDataSource = feedbacks
  90 │         for (const feedback of this.feedbackDataSource) {
→ 91 │           feedback.comment = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(feedback.comment)
  92 │         }
  93 │         this.feedbackDataSource = new MatTableDataSource(this.feedbackDataSource)
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-184](#m-184) — Remove sanitizer bypass and render feedback as text

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)


<a id="t-079"></a><a id="f-079"></a>
#### F-079 · Forgeable continue codes

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/restoreProgress.ts:18`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** The continue-code salts are hardcoded in source and the apply routes are unauthenticated. Anyone can encode arbitrary challenge ids with the public salt and PUT `/rest/continue-code/apply/`&lt;code&gt; to mark challenges solved on a shared CTF instance.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-221](#m-221) — Sign continue codes with a secret key

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-111"></a><a id="f-111"></a>
#### F-111 · Hardcoded cookie signing secret in server setup

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:289`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** At `server.ts:289`, `cookieParser` is initialised with the string literal 'kekse' as the cookie signing secret. Anyone who reads the public source knows the secret and can forge signed cookies that the server accepts as genuine, wherever signed cookies are relied upon. No control is in place.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-253](#m-253) — Move cookie signing secret to external configuration

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

#### F-115 · Hardcoded test account credentials in client bundle

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Literals are present, but `routes/login.ts:65` only marks a challenge solved; it does not show the account exists in the Users table on a deployment.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** The login component ships the email and password of a test account in the public JavaScript bundle. Anyone can read them from the bundle and log in as that account. `routes/login.ts:65` confirms that this pair is a valid login.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `login.component.ts:61-62` define `testingUsername` and `testingPassword` literals. `routes/login.ts:65` recognizes the same pair, and line 34 authenticates it against the Users table.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-157](#m-157) — Manual review: verify Hardcoded test account credentials in client bundle · ◑ [M-257](#m-257) — Remove embedded test credentials from frontend code

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-116"></a><a id="f-116"></a>
#### F-116 · Hardcoded test account credentials in the client bundle

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** The login component hardcodes a test account email and plaintext password as the public fields `testingUsername` and `testingPassword`, and they ship in the JavaScript bundle. Anyone who downloads the bundle can read them and log in as that account if it exists on the deployment.

Any visitor can learn a reusable account credential; if the server accepts it, they get authenticated access as the test account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:61-62` assign `testingUsername` and `testingPassword`, an embedded password credential, in the shipped Angular component.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-258](#m-258) — Remove embedded test credentials from client code

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-065"></a><a id="f-065"></a>
#### F-065 · Server trusts unauthenticated client-reported

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-2  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → Real-time WebSocket Channel · Authentication: State-changing verify events are accepted across the Socket\.IO boundary from clients that were never identified.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-2 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Issue:** An attacker connects without credentials and emits `verifyLocalXssChallenge` with the literal expected payload string, or emits `verifyCloseNotificationsChallenge` with any array of two or more items. The server calls `solveIf` and persists `solved=true` without ever seeing the attack performed, which spoofs a legitimate solve and corrupts scoreboard and webhook state.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Lines 40-51 register verify* handlers on unauthenticated sockets. Each calls `challengeUtils.solveIf`, which calls `solve()`, sets `challenge.solved` = true and saves it.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-210](#m-210) — Verify challenge solutions server-side with authenticated sockets

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-066"></a><a id="f-066"></a>
#### F-066 · Product reviews created anonymously

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-1  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/createProductReviews.ts:26`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express Server · Authentication: An anonymous ingress route writes review data with a client-asserted author, which breaks the authentication leg.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-1 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Issue:** PUT `/rest/products/:id/reviews` is registered without `isAuthorized` (`server.ts:633`). `createProductReviews` stores `req.body.author` verbatim, so an anonymous attacker can post reviews in any customer's name. Such a review can also carry the indirect prompt-injection payload described in -004.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◕ [M-211](#m-211) — Require authentication and derive review author server-side

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-067"></a><a id="f-067"></a>
#### F-067 · Reset-password rate limit keyed on spoofable forwarded address

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:346`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-012](#w-012) - Reset-password rate limit keyed on spoofable forwarded address (`server.ts:346`)

**Issue:** With trust proxy enabled, `req.ip` is taken from X-Forwarded-For; the limiter keys on that client-controlled value, so an attacker rotates the header to brute-force security answers on `/rest/user/reset-password`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-212](#m-212) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (🟡 [F-067](#f-067))

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-068"></a><a id="f-068"></a>
#### F-068 · No minimum password strength on registration

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:19`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Registration and change-password reject only empty passwords or a mismatched repeat, so users can set one-character or common passwords. An attacker can then guess them through the login endpoint, which has no rate limiter in the inspected lines.

Weak passwords are easy to guess, which leads to account takeover.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `changePassword.ts:19-25` and `server.ts:408-418` have no length, complexity or breached-password check before the password is stored.

**Fix:** Enforce a length and complexity policy and reject reused / breached passwords → ◑ [M-213](#m-213) — Enforce a server-side password strength policy

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-521](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/521.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-077"></a><a id="f-077"></a>
#### F-077 · Wallet top-up amount accepted without payment or bounds

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-B-005 (User exceeds a limit or a one-time entitlement); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/wallet.ts:27`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An authenticated user sends PUT `/rest/wallet/balance` with one of their own card ids as `paymentId` and an arbitrary balance. `addWalletBalance` checks only that the card exists for the user (`wallet.ts:24`).

It then increments the wallet by `req.body.balance` (`wallet.ts:27`) without charging the card or validating the sign or size of the amount, which mints store credit.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-219](#m-219) — Validate amount and settle payment before crediting

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-20](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-081"></a><a id="f-081"></a>
#### F-081 · Remote image body stored unchecked

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:30`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-043](#w-043) - Remote image body stored unchecked and unbounded (`profileImageUrlUpload.ts:30`)

**Issue:** A logged-in user points `imageUrl` at a large or script-bearing resource ending in .svg. The server streams the whole body, with no size or content check, into the public uploads directory as {id}.svg, where it is served from the application origin.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Validate uploaded file type, size, and storage path; never execute uploaded content → ◑ [M-223](#m-223) — Validate and size-limit fetched image content

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02)

<a id="t-082"></a><a id="f-082"></a>
#### F-082 · Expired campaign coupon accepted

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Instances (2):** `routes/order.ts`: 201; `frontend/src/app/payment/payment.component.ts`: 160

**Issue:** At checkout the attacker sends `couponData`=`base64('WMNSDY2019-&lt;validOn ms&gt;')`. The server compares the client-supplied date with `campaign.validOn` instead of the current time, so expired 40-75% campaign discounts are applied to any order.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-224](#m-224) — Validate campaign window against server clock

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-083"></a><a id="f-083"></a>
#### F-083 · Unauthenticated product modification

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-1  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express Server · Authentication: A state-changing product update is reachable at this unauthenticated ingress without any token check.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-1 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Instances (2):** `server.ts`: 485, 497

**Issue:** The PUT guard for `/api/Products/:id` is commented out (`server.ts:370`), and no other middleware covers that path. The finale Product resource (`server.ts:485`, endpoints at 503) therefore accepts PUT from anonymous callers. POST `/api/Products` requires only a login, not an admin role.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◕ [M-225](#m-225) — Guard product write endpoints with admin authorization

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-084"></a><a id="f-084"></a>
#### F-084 · Unauthenticated review creation takes

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/createProductReviews.ts:26`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Any caller sends PUT `/rest/products/1/reviews` with author set to another user's e-mail. The route has no `isAuthorized` guard, and the handler stores `req.body.author` verbatim, so the attacker can post reviews in the victim's name.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:633` registers the route without an authorization guard. `createProductReviews.ts:16-20` notice a forged author but proceed, and line 26 inserts author from the body.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-226](#m-226) — Derive the review author from the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-085"></a><a id="f-085"></a>
#### F-085 · Feedback creation trusts a body-supplied UserId

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:402`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A caller posts to `/api/Feedbacks` with UserId set to another user's id. The `forgedFeedbackChallenge` middleware detects the mismatch but only records a challenge and calls `next()`. The generated Feedback resource then stores the feedback attributed to the victim.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `verify.ts:33` compares `body.UserId` with the session user but does not block the request. `server.ts:486` exposes Feedback through finale with no exclusions. `models/feedback.ts:31` declares UserId as a plain writable attribute.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-227](#m-227) — Set feedback UserId from the authenticated session

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-086"></a><a id="f-086"></a>
#### F-086 · NoSQL operator injection in review update and like selectors

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-8](#tb-8) - Juice Shop Express Server → MarsDB Store · Query construction: The request-body id is passed to `reviewsCollection.findOne` as the _id criterion with no type check, so an operator object can reach MarsDB query interpretation.

**Instances (2):** `routes/likeProductReviews.ts`: 25; `routes/updateProductReviews.ts`: 18

**Issue:** At `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`, an authenticated user sends PATCH `/rest/products/reviews` with a JSON object id such as `{"id":{"$ne":-1},"message":"x"}` or id[\$ne]=x through extended urlencoded parsing. The object becomes the MarsDB _id selector and `multi:true` is set, so the attacker overwrites the message of every review, including other users' reviews.

That handler requires an authenticated user (lines 19-21) but has no type check on id.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◑ [M-228](#m-228) — Use parameterized database queries (🟡 [F-086](#f-086))

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02)

<a id="t-087"></a><a id="f-087"></a>
#### F-087 · Stored product reviews returned as tool results enable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:149`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-020](#w-020) - Stored product reviews returned as tool results enable indirect injection

**Issue:** LLM01 / ASI01: anyone can write review messages through the unauthenticated PUT `/rest/products/:id/reviews` (`createProductReviews` inserts `req.body.message`). `getProductReviews` returns those messages to the model as tool output, with no marking that separates data from instructions.

Usernames from the profile are also interpolated into the system prompt at `buildSystemPrompt`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-229](#m-229) — Treat retrieved reviews as untrusted data in model context

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-091"></a><a id="f-091"></a>
#### F-091 · Unauthenticated websocket events alter global

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:33`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Issue:** Any client can open a socket\.io connection without credentials. CORS does not restrict non-browser clients. The client can emit 'notification received' to delete challenge notifications for all users, or emit verify* events to mark challenges solved globally.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-233](#m-233) — Authenticate socket connections before handling events

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-092"></a><a id="f-092"></a>
#### F-092 · Client-controlled header recorded as last login IP

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/saveLoginIp.ts:18`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An authenticated user calls GET `/rest/saveLoginIp` with an arbitrary True-Client-IP header. The handler stores it as the account's `lastLoginIp`, so the login-IP record shown to users and admins can be forged. When the `httpHeaderXss` challenge is enabled, the value is stored without sanitization.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-234](#m-234) — Derive login IP from the trusted socket address

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Repudiation · [CWE-348](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/348.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

<a id="t-093"></a><a id="f-093"></a>
#### F-093 · Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-2  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → Real-time WebSocket Channel · Authentication: Flags are emitted across the internet-facing Socket\.IO boundary to connections that were never authenticated.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-2 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Instances (2):** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts`: 30, 36

**Issue:** An attacker opens a Socket\.IO connection from a non-browser client, which CORS does not constrain. The connection handler replays every stored notification without any authentication.

Each notification includes the CTF flag that `sendNotification` computes with `utils.ctfFlag`, so the attacker collects flags for challenges that other participants solved.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The `io.on('connection')` handler (line 23) has no auth check and emits each entry of notifications (line 30). Each entry contains flag: `utils.ctfFlag(challenge.name)` (`challengeUtils.ts:52`,66).

**Fix:** ◕ [M-235](#m-235) — Authenticate socket handshake and omit flags

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-094"></a><a id="f-094"></a>
#### F-094 · Local file read

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/dataErasure.ts:104`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: `req.body` is spread into `res.render` options after a weak denylist, but the view engine's use of layout as a file is not in the supplied source.

**Issue:** A logged-in user sends POST `/dataerasure` with a body field layout set to a server file path. The handler resolves the path and checks it only against the substrings ftp, `ctf.key` and encryptionkeys.

It then spreads `req.body` into the `res.render` options, so the view engine loads the chosen file as the layout and returns its first 100 characters.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-153](#m-153) — Manual review: verify Local file read through user-controlled template layout option · ◑ [M-236](#m-236) — Stop passing request body into render options

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-73](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/73.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-095"></a><a id="f-095"></a>
#### F-095 · FTP file-type allow-list checked before null-byte

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileServer.ts:28`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An anonymous caller requests `/ftp/package.json.bak%2500.md`. The name passes the .md allow-list and is then cut at the poison null byte, so a backup file that the listing at `/ftp` reveals is served despite the md/pdf restriction.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-237](#m-237) — Canonicalize file name before enforcing type allow-list

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-180](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/180.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-096"></a><a id="f-096"></a>
#### F-096 · Captcha endpoint returns the expected answer

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/captcha.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker calls GET `/rest/captcha`. The response contains the answer field, which `verifyCaptcha` later compares against. The attacker replays `captchaId` and answer to submit feedback automatically, which defeats the CAPTCHA.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-238](#m-238) — Omit the captcha answer from the response

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-097"></a><a id="f-097"></a>
#### F-097 · Image captcha endpoint returns the expected answer

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/imageCaptcha.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** A logged-in attacker calls GET `/rest/image-captcha`. The response includes answer, the text that `verifyImageCaptcha` checks before a data export. The attacker scripts data exports without solving the CAPTCHA.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-239](#m-239) — Return only the captcha image to the client

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-098"></a><a id="f-098"></a>
#### F-098 · Security question lookup discloses questions

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/securityQuestion.ts:23`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** At `routes/securityQuestion.ts:23`, an unauthenticated attacker calls GET `/rest/user/security-question?email=``&lt;candidate&gt;`. A registered email with a security answer returns its question, while an unknown email returns {}.

Account enumeration supports targeted credential stuffing and guessing of security answers during reset.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `securityQuestion.ts:21-25` returns {question} for a matching email and {} otherwise. `server.ts:599` registers the route with no guard.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-240](#m-240) — Return a uniform response from the security question lookup

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-204](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/204.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05)

<a id="t-099"></a><a id="f-099"></a>
#### F-099 · Whoami fields parameter returns password hash

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/currentUser.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** The caller supplies ?`fields=password`,`totpSecret`,`deluxeToken`. The handler copies any requested attribute of the session's user object into the response, with no allowlist, and also offers a JSONP callback.

Script running in the victim's context, or a cross-origin JSONP include that carries the token cookie, can read the password hash and TOTP secret.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-241](#m-241) — Allowlist the fields that whoami may return

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-100"></a><a id="f-100"></a>
#### F-100 · User list exposes all users' email, role, deluxeToken and last

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:484`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An attacker registers an ordinary customer account and calls GET `/api/Users` with the bearer token. The generated finale resource returns every user record and leaves out only password and `totpSecret`.

Any authenticated user can collect the identity data of every account: emails, roles, membership tokens and IP addresses.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:363` registers only `isAuthorized` for GET `/api/Users`. `server.ts:484` excludes only password and `totpSecret`. `models/user.ts:100-104` defines the `deluxeToken` and `lastLoginIp` fields that are returned.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-242](#m-242) — Restrict user listing to admins and return minimal fields

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-101"></a><a id="f-101"></a>
#### F-101 · Confidential business rule embedded in extractable system

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:105`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** LLM07 - the system prompt contains a confidential 15% courtesy discount rule; since callers control history (004), they can extract it and use it to obtain discounts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-243](#m-243) — Move confidential rules out of the system prompt

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-497](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/497.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-102"></a><a id="f-102"></a>
#### F-102 · Access logs browsable and downloadable without authentication

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:283`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Anonymous directory listing of logs is shown, but `serveLogFiles` handler and log contents are not in supplied source, so download is unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-039](#w-039) - Access logs browsable and downloadable without authentication (`server.ts:283`)

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker browses `/support/logs` and downloads `access.log` files. These contain client IPs, request URLs with query strings, and user agents of other users.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Strip secrets and PII from every log sink and rotate any token that already leaked → ◑ [M-154](#m-154) — Manual review: verify Access logs browsable and downloadable without authentication · ◑ [M-244](#m-244) — Remove public log directory and file routes

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-532](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/532.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

<a id="t-103"></a><a id="f-103"></a>
#### F-103 · Order confirmation PDFs written to publicly listed ftp

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/order.ts:45`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: PDFs with the customer email are written to ftp/, but the supplied source does not show that ftp/ is served with directory listing (`server.ts:269-270` not provided).

**Weakness:** [W-045](#w-045) - Order confirmation PDFs written to publicly listed ftp directory (`order.ts:45`)

**Issue:** Every checkout writes order_&lt;id&gt;.pdf, containing the customer email and the items bought, into ftp/. The ftp/ folder is served with `serveIndex` and public file download (`server.ts:269-270`), so anyone can list and download other customers' order confirmations.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-155](#m-155) — Manual review: verify Order confirmation PDFs written to publicly listed ftp directory · ◑ [M-245](#m-245) — Store order PDFs outside web-served directories

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-552](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/552.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-104"></a><a id="f-104"></a>
#### F-104 · Encryption keys directory listed and served anonymously

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:277`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** At `server.ts:277`, `/encryptionkeys` is listed by `serveIndex` and its files are served through `serveKeyFiles`. An anonymous caller browses the listing and downloads any file there; `jwt.pub` is confirmed present.

The only control is a check that file names do not contain '/' (`keyServer.ts` 13); there is no authentication and no file allowlist.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-246](#m-246) — Stop serving the encryptionkeys directory publicly

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-552](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/552.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-105"></a><a id="f-105"></a>
#### F-105 · Access logs browsable and downloadable without authentication

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:281`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-046](#w-046) - Access logs browsable and downloadable without authentication (`server.ts:281`)

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker browses `/support/logs` and downloads the access log files through `serveLogFiles`. No guard runs on these routes. The logs reveal client IPs and request URLs, including credentials sent in query strings.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-247](#m-247) — Remove public log routes or restrict them to admins

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-552](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/552.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05)

<a id="t-106"></a><a id="f-106"></a>
#### F-106 · Server log directory listed and served anonymously

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:281`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An anonymous caller browses `/support/logs` and downloads access logs through `serveLogFiles`. The logs show other users' IPs, request URLs and parameters.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-248](#m-248) — Remove public log browsing and download routes

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-552](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/552.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-107"></a><a id="f-107"></a>
#### F-107 · Change password sends current and new passwords

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:14`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-040](#w-040) - Change password sends current and new passwords in GET query string (`changePassw`

**Issue:** At `routes/changePassword.ts:14`, GET `/rest/user/change-password` reads the current, new and repeat passwords from URL query parameters. URLs are recorded in the Morgan combined access logs, proxy logs and browser history.

The route requires a bearer token, but nothing protects the parameter location.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-249](#m-249) — Send password change credentials in POST body

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04)

<a id="t-108"></a><a id="f-108"></a>
#### F-108 · Poison null byte bypasses file type allowlist on FTP

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileServer.ts:28`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Allowlist runs before `cutOffPoisonNullByte`, but that helper's behavior and upstream auth on `/ftp` are not in the supplied source.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker requests `/ftp/package.json.bak%2500.md`. The .md suffix passes the allowlist, then `cutOffPoisonNullByte` truncates the name at %00, and `res.sendFile` serves the forbidden backup file.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-156](#m-156) — Manual review: verify Poison null byte bypasses file type allowlist on FTP downloads · ◑ [M-250](#m-250) — Validate filename after normalization

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-626](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/626.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-109"></a><a id="f-109"></a>
#### F-109 · Orders looked up by non-unique vowel-masked e-mail

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Instances (2):** `routes/dataExport.ts`: 33; `routes/orderHistory.ts`: 17

**Issue:** Orders are looked up by the caller's e-mail with every vowel replaced by '*'. That key is not unique. The orders query does not use the user id.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-251](#m-251) — Query orders by authenticated user id

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-110"></a><a id="f-110"></a>
#### F-110 · User directory exposed to any authenticated user

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-3  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:363`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-3](#tb-3) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express Server · Authorization: The route is authenticated but has no object- or role-level authorization against the authenticated user.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-3 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Issue:** At `server.ts:363`, any self-registered customer with a valid JWT can call GET `/api/Users` or GET `/api/Users/:id`. The only guard is `security.isAuthorized()` (`server.ts:363` and 365). Only password and `totpSecret` are excluded (`server.ts:484`).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-252](#m-252) — Restrict user listing to admins and own record

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-112"></a><a id="f-112"></a>
#### F-112 · Passwords verified and stored as unsalted fast

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.
