**Weakness:** [W-047](#w-047) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** Change-password compares `security.hash(current)` with the stored password by plain equality, so passwords are kept as unsalted deterministic digests (`MD5` in the shared helper). An attacker who obtains the Users table, for example through SQL injection, can crack most of them quickly with GPU brute force or rainbow tables.

Cracked plaintext passwords allow account takeover here and on other sites where users reuse them.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `changePassword.ts:39` compares `security.hash(currentPassword)` with the stored password value. The shared helper and the User model setter apply the same unsalted `MD5` digest.

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◑ [M-254](#m-254) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm (🟡 [F-112](#f-112))

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-113"></a><a id="f-113"></a>
#### F-113 · Current and new passwords sent in a GET query string

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:54`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07), [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-041](#w-041) - Current and new passwords sent in a GET query string (`user.service.ts:54`)

**Issue:** `changePassword` sends GET `/rest/user/change-password?current=`...&`new=`...&`repeat=`... with the passwords as query parameters, and the survey notes the values are not even URL-encoded. Anyone with access to those logs, or to a shared device's history, can collect the user's password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `user.service.ts` lines 54-55 build '/`rest/user/change-password?current`=' + `passwords.current` + '&new=' + `passwords.new` + '&repeat=' + ... and send it via `http.get`.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-255](#m-255) — Send password changes as a POST request body

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07), [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04)

<a id="t-114"></a><a id="f-114"></a>
#### F-114 · OAuth access token carried in URL fragment and query string

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:68`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07), [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-042](#w-042) - OAuth access token carried in URL fragment and query string (`user.service.ts:68`)

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-10](#tb-10) - Angular Frontend → external · Egress content: The access token crosses the browser-to-Google boundary in the URL query, which breaks the egress-content assumption.

**Issue:** `oauthLogin` appends the Google access token to the userinfo URL as an `access_token` query parameter. The token can then end up in proxy logs, browser history and monitoring tools. The requests use HTTPS to a fixed Google host.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `user.service.ts:68` calls `'https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v1/userinfo?alt=json&access_token='` + `accessToken`.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-256](#m-256) — Send the access token in the Authorization header

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07), [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04)

<a id="t-119"></a><a id="f-119"></a>
#### F-119 · Login IP endpoint returns the full user record

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/saveLoginIp.ts:33`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** Each call to GET `/rest/saveLoginIp` returns the whole updated User model, including the `MD5` password hash, `totpSecret` and `deluxeToken`. A script that rides on the session, such as an XSS payload, can read these values and crack the hash or clone the TOTP generator.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-261](#m-261) — Return only the saved IP from saveLoginIp

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-125"></a><a id="f-125"></a>
#### F-125 · Unbounded in-memory wallet set growth

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/web3Wallet.ts:16`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Unbounded Set add with no validation is shown, but global rate limiting or body-size limits were not inspected, so DoS impact is unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-017](#w-017) - Unbounded in-memory wallet set growth from unauthenticated requests

**Issue:** An attacker repeatedly POSTs `/rest/web3/walletExploitAddress`, each time with a different or large `walletAddress` value. Every value is added to the module-level `walletsConnected` Set.

Entries are removed only when a matching ContractExploited event arrives, so server memory grows until the process degrades or crashes.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - Line 15 reads `req.body.walletAddress`, and line 16 calls `walletsConnected.add` with no type, format, length or count check. `server.ts:645` registers the route without auth. Global rate limiting in `server.ts` was not inspected.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-158](#m-158) — Manual review: verify Unbounded in-memory wallet set growth from unauthenticated requests · ◑ [M-267](#m-267) — Validate addresses and cap the connected-wallet set

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-126"></a><a id="f-126"></a>
#### F-126 · Synchronous XML and YAML parsing blocks event loop

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:109`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker repeatedly uploads YAML alias bombs or XML entity-expansion documents to `/file-upload`. Each one runs synchronously in `vm.runInContext` for up to 2 seconds, blocking the Node event loop. Parallel requests stall the whole server.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-268](#m-268) — Bound parser resource consumption

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-776](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/776.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-131"></a><a id="f-131"></a>
#### F-131 · Any authenticated user can delete any customer feedback

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:361`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A registered customer sends DELETE `/api/Feedbacks/:id` for feedback written by other users. The only guard is `isAuthorized`, which checks for a validly signed JWT.

Neither the owner (`Feedback.UserId`) nor an admin role is checked, so the customer can delete arbitrary feedback, an operation the admin UI offers.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts` line 361 applies only `isAuthorized` to `/api/Feedbacks/:id` before the finale resource at line 501. Feedback carries a UserId owner (`models/feedback.ts` line 31) that is never compared with the caller.

**Fix:** Add explicit server-side authorisation checks on every protected route → ◑ [M-271](#m-271) — Enforce admin role or ownership on feedback deletion

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-285](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/285.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-132"></a><a id="f-132"></a>
#### F-132 · Any authenticated user can create catalogue products

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:369`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A customer sends POST `/api/Products` with any name, price, `deluxePrice` and description. Only `isAuthorized` guards the route, so the generated create handler adds the product to the shop catalogue that all users see.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Add explicit server-side authorisation checks on every protected route → ◑ [M-272](#m-272) — Restrict product creation to an admin role

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-285](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/285.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-133"></a><a id="f-133"></a>
#### F-133 · Duplicate JSON keys bypass BasketId ownership check

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:37`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** `addBasketItem` parses the raw body with clarinet and keeps every duplicate key. The ownership check compares only `basketIds[0]` with `user.bid`, but the stored item uses `basketIds[basketIds.length-1]`.

The quantity limit check reads `req.body.quantity`, which can also differ from the stored last value.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-273](#m-273) — Derive BasketId from session and reject duplicate keys

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-134"></a><a id="f-134"></a>
#### F-134 · Basket item creation lets a duplicate BasketId target

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:42`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated attacker posts a JSON body to `/api/BasketItems` that repeats the BasketId key: first their own basket id, then the victim's. The check compares only the first value with `user.bid`, but the item is built with the last value, so the product is added to the victim's basket.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 37 checks `basketIds[0]` against `user.bid`. Line 42 uses `basketIds[basketIds.length - 1]` for the saved BasketItem at line 47.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-274](#m-274) — Derive BasketId from the authenticated session

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-135"></a><a id="f-135"></a>
#### F-135 · Review update lacks author ownership check

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:17`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated user can edit any other user's review by sending its _id. The handler looks up the caller but does not include author: `user.data.email` in the update selector.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-275](#m-275) — Scope review updates to the authenticated author

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-136"></a><a id="f-136"></a>
#### F-136 · Any authenticated user can edit other users'

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - verified attack-chain keystone in MODEL-AC-003 (User posts or edits product reviews as another user); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated attacker sends PATCH `/rest/products/reviews` with the _id of another user's review in `body.id` and a new message. The handler updates the review matching that _id without comparing its author to the caller, so the attacker rewrites the victim's review.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:634` registers the route behind `isAuthorized` only. `updateProductReviews.ts:16` resolves the user but does not use it, and line 18 filters only on `req.body.id`.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-276](#m-276) — Bind review updates to the review author

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-137"></a><a id="f-137"></a>
#### F-137 · Basket items readable and deletable across users by id

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:359`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A logged-in user iterates ids on GET and DELETE `/api/BasketItems/:id`. Both routes are guarded only by `isAuthorized`, and the finale-generated handlers look the item up by primary key alone. The user can read or remove items in other customers' baskets.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `/api/BasketItems/:id` is guarded only by `security.isAuthorized()` (`server.ts` line 359) before `finale.resource` (line 501). BasketItem links to its owner only through BasketId (`models/basketitem.ts` line 31), which is never checked against the caller's basket.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-277](#m-277) — Bind basket item access to the caller's basket

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-138"></a><a id="f-138"></a>
#### F-138 · Any authenticated user can read any user record by id

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:365`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A logged-in customer calls GET `/api/Users/1`, /2 and so on. `isAuthorized` only checks that the JWT is valid, then the finale read returns the full record of the requested user: email, role, `deluxeToken`, `lastLoginIp` and `profileImage`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts` 365 guards GET `/api/Users/:id` only with `isAuthorized`. Lines 484 and 503 expose the User model at `/api/Users/:id` through finale, with no read hook comparing the id to the caller.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-278](#m-278) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟡 [F-138](#f-138))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-139"></a><a id="f-139"></a>
#### F-139 · Deluxe membership granted without payment for unknown

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-06](#tc-authz-06))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** At `routes/deluxe.ts:43`, a logged-in customer sends POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` with `paymentMode` set to any value other than wallet or card, or with an empty body {} so no `paymentMode` is set. Neither payment branch runs, and the handler updates the user's role to deluxe and issues a newly signed token without payment.

`appendUserId` binds the request to the caller, and the wallet balance and card expiry are checked only inside their own branches.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-279](#m-279) — Reject unknown payment modes before upgrade

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-841](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/841.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-06](#tc-authz-06)

<a id="t-140"></a><a id="f-140"></a>
#### F-140 · Server access logs are downloadable without any authorization

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:283`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: No auth precedes the log routes, but `verify.accessControlChallenges()` at line 282 is not shown, so whether it blocks requests is unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An anonymous attacker browses `/support/logs` and downloads `access.log` files through `/support/logs/:file`. The route is registered before any authentication middleware, so the attacker gains operator-only access to request logs that hold client IPs, URLs and user agents.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `server.ts:331` writes morgan logs to logs/access.log.%DATE%. `server.ts:281-283` exposes the logs directory listing and files with no guard. `logfileServer.ts:14` sends the files.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-161](#m-161) — Manual review: verify Server access logs are downloadable without any authorization · ◑ [M-280](#m-280) — Restrict log file access to administrators

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-141"></a><a id="f-141"></a>
#### F-141 · Deluxe membership granted without payment verification

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A logged-in customer sends POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` with `paymentMode` omitted or set to any value other than wallet or card. Both payment checks are skipped, and the user is upgraded to the deluxe role for free.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `deluxe.ts` lines 24 and 34 check payment only when `paymentMode` is 'wallet' or 'card'. Line 43 grants the deluxe role unconditionally.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-281](#m-281) — Reject deluxe upgrades without a verified payment

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-142"></a><a id="f-142"></a>
#### F-142 · Client-supplied UserId accepted on feedback and complaint

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:486`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03), [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** An attacker sends POST `/api/Feedbacks`, which needs no authentication, or POST `/api/Complaints`, which needs any login, with a body UserId that belongs to another user. The finale resources, and at line 489 for complaints, save the body as sent.

The survey notes that the model files were not read, so it cannot rule out an override at the model level.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts` 486 registers Feedback with exclude []. `models/feedback.ts` 31 defines UserId as a plain field. `routes/verify.ts` 33 shows that a body UserId different from the caller passes through to `next()`.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-282](#m-282) — Bind feedback UserId to the authenticated caller

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03), [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-143"></a><a id="f-143"></a>
#### F-143 · Security answer creation accepts owner UserId from request body

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to Critical: reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:501`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** An anonymous caller sends POST `/api/SecurityAnswers` with an arbitrary UserId, SecurityQuestionId and answer. The route has no authentication and does not bind UserId to the caller, so the attacker can set the password-recovery answer for any account that has none yet and then reset that account's password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - SecurityAnswer is a finale resource (`server.ts` lines 492, 501). Only GET and /:id are guarded (lines 395-396). The UserId owner field (`models/securityAnswer.ts` line 29) is taken from the body. The attack needs a target account without an existing answer, which the served source does not show.

**Fix:** ● [M-283](#m-283) — Bind security answers to the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-144"></a><a id="f-144"></a>
#### F-144 · Chatbot coupon tool issues any model-chosen discount

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AI-01](#tc-ai-01), [TC-AI-03](#tc-ai-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An anonymous caller sends POST `/rest/chat` with messages that inject instructions, such as claiming eligibility or demanding a 90% coupon. The model calls `generateCoupon` with that discount.

The server runs `security.generateCoupon(discount)` with no cap, order verification or caller authorization, and returns a valid coupon code that can be redeemed at checkout.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-284](#m-284) — Enforce coupon policy in the tool server-side

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AI-01](#tc-ai-01), [TC-AI-03](#tc-ai-03)

<a id="t-145"></a><a id="f-145"></a>
#### F-145 · Client-side role guards trust unverified JWT claims

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:54`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-024](#w-024) - Client-side role guards trust unverified JWT claims (`app.guard.ts:54`)

**Issue:** AdminGuard, AccountingGuard and DeluxeGuard decode the JWT from `localStorage` with `jwtDecode` (no signature check) and grant access when `payload.data.role` matches. A user can place a forged token with role 'admin' in `localStorage` to open the administration and accounting views; any protection of the underlying data therefore depends entirely on server-side authorization, which is not shown in this job.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `app.guard.ts:38` calls `jwtDecode` on the `localStorage` token without verification and :54/:70/:86 make role decisions on the decoded claim; LoginGuard (:18) only checks token presence. `app.routing.ts:81` applies AdminGuard to the administration route.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-285](#m-285) — Enforce authorization on the server (🟡 [F-145](#f-145))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-070"></a><a id="f-070"></a>
#### F-070 · JWT verification accepts the algorithm from the token

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-4  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:52`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-4](#tb-4) 🌐 External - external → Authentication &amp; Session Surface · Authentication: The finding's evidence line is the interface this boundary cites; CWE-347 breaks its authentication condition.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-4 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Issue:** An attacker crafts a token with an algorithm other than `RS256` in its header, such as `HS256` signed with the public key as the HMAC secret, or none. Because `isAuthorized`, `security.verify` and `updateAuthenticatedUsers` pass no algorithm allow-list, the verifying library may accept it depending on its version, letting the attacker impersonate any user.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `expressJwt({ secret: publicKey })`, `jws.verify(token, publicKey)` and `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, cb)` are all called without pinning `RS256`.

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◕ [M-215](#m-215) — Pin RS256 in every token verification call

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02)

<a id="t-071"></a><a id="f-071"></a>
#### F-071 · Token verification helper never checks expiry

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** `security.verify` uses `jws.verify`, which checks only the signature. `isAccounting`, `isDeluxe`, `isCustomer`, the 2FA `tmpToken` check and the 2FA `setupToken` check all accept a token after its exp has passed. A stolen `tmpToken` or an accounting token therefore stays usable indefinitely.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - verify = `jws.verify(token, publicKey)` and its result is followed by `decode()` with no check of exp, iss or aud (`routes/2fa.ts:20`, `lib/insecurity.ts:156`).

**Fix:** ◑ [M-216](#m-216) — Validate exp, iss and aud in the verify helper

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03)

<a id="t-117"></a><a id="f-117"></a>
#### F-117 · Authentication-details endpoint exposes all

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/authenticatedUsers.ts:25`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An attacker with any valid customer account calls GET `/rest/user/authentication-details`. The response lists every user's email, role, `lastLoginIp`, `deluxeToken` and profile data. Admin and accounting accounts can be enumerated this way.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-259](#m-259) — Restrict and minimise authentication-details output

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-118"></a><a id="f-118"></a>
#### F-118 · Session JWT payload carries password hash and TOTP secret

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/2fa.ts:42`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Login and 2FA verification sign the full user row into the session JWT, which is returned to the browser and also set as a token cookie. Any party that obtains the token can base64-decode it and read the `MD5` password hash, and the TOTP seed after 2FA login.

Such parties include injected scripts, logs and proxies.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-260](#m-260) — Limit JWT claims to non-sensitive identifiers

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-201](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/201.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05)

<a id="t-122"></a><a id="f-122"></a>
#### F-122 · Session token cookie set without HttpOnly or Secure

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:192`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-030](#w-030) - Sensitive cookies remain accessible to browser scripts

**Issue:** `updateAuthenticatedUsers` sets `res.cookie('token', token)` with default options, so the bearer JWT is readable by page JavaScript. Any XSS in the application can steal a valid 6-hour session token, and the server-side `tokenMap` has no revocation path.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Set `HttpOnly` on every session cookie → ◑ [M-264](#m-264) — Set secure session-cookie attributes

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-1004](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1004.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-123"></a><a id="f-123"></a>
#### F-123 · Payment card numbers stored unencrypted in SQLite

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/card.ts:38`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-034](#w-034) - Payment card numbers stored unencrypted in SQLite (`card.ts:38`)

**Issue:** An attacker who reads data/juiceshop.sqlite, for example through a file disclosure, a backup or SQL injection, gets full 16-digit card numbers with holder names and expiry dates. The Cards model stores `cardNum` as a plain INTEGER column with no encryption.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Encrypt the data in transit and at rest with vetted primitives → ◑ [M-265](#m-265) — Encrypt or tokenize stored card numbers

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02)

<a id="t-127"></a><a id="f-127"></a>
#### F-127 · No brute-force throttling on login and password reset

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:32`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: The login handler and the route at `server.ts:596` show no limiter, but global middleware was not inspected, so the absence of throttling is unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-013](#w-013) - No brute-force throttling on login and password reset (`login.ts:32`)

**Issue:** The login handler performs a credential query on every request with no attempt counter, lockout or route-level rate limiter. An attacker can spray or brute-force passwords, and likewise guess security answers on reset-password, at network speed.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-159](#m-159) — Manual review: verify No brute-force throttling on login and password reset · ◑ [M-269](#m-269) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (🟡 [F-127](#f-127))

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-146"></a><a id="f-146"></a>
#### F-146 · Missing role check on authenticated user

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/authenticatedUsers.ts:12`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** Any registered customer with a valid JWT calls GET `/rest/user/authentication-details`. The guard only verifies the token, and the handler returns every user's record with all fields except masked password and `totpSecret: emails`, roles, `deluxeToken`, `lastLoginIp` and `lastLoginTime`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-286](#m-286) — Restrict user listing to the admin role

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-200"></a><a id="f-200"></a>
#### F-200 · Shopper tampers with another customer's basket or order

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/Services/basket.service.ts:56`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** Client sends a caller-chosen basket id to `/rest/basket/{id}` and `/checkout`, and `BasketItem.quantity` has no validator. Server handlers that would enforce basket ownership are not in the readable sources, so the boundary can't be decided.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-162](#m-162) — Manual review: verify Shopper tampers with another customer's basket or order contents · ◑ [M-287](#m-287) — Verify basket and order ownership on every access

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-064"></a><a id="f-064"></a>
#### F-064 · Open redirect

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Instances (2):** `routes/redirect.ts`: 19; `lib/insecurity.ts`: 136

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link such as `/redirect?to`=`https://evil.example/?x=https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop`. `isRedirectAllowed` passes the target because it uses `url.includes` against allowlisted URLs.

`performRedirect` then calls `res.redirect(toUrl)`, which sends the victim from the trusted shop domain to the attacker's phishing site.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `redirect.ts:15` takes `query.to`, and line 16 gates it with `security.isRedirectAllowed`. `lib/insecurity.ts:136` implements that check as `url.includes(allowedUrl)`, so any URL that contains an allowlisted string passes. Line 19 redirects to the URL. `server.ts:659` registers GET `/redirect` without auth.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-209](#m-209) — Validate redirect targets against an allowlist

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-069"></a><a id="f-069"></a>
#### F-069 · OAuth implicit flow without state, nonce or PKCE

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-06](#tc-authn-06))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-026](#w-026) - OAuth sign-in requests are not bound to the user's browser session

**Issue:** `googleLogin` starts the OAuth implicit flow (`response_type=token`) with no state, nonce or PKCE binding. An attacker gets a Google access token for their own account for this client and lures the victim to the app URL with #`access_token`=&lt;attacker token&gt; appended.

The survey places this check lines 80-87 and verification at lines 80-83.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:148` builds the authorize URL with `response_type=token` and no state or `code_challenge`. `oauth.component.ts:28` and 70-78 take `access_token` from the fragment without checking any state.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◑ [M-214](#m-214) — Use authorization code flow with PKCE and state

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-06](#tc-authn-06)

<a id="t-078"></a><a id="f-078"></a>
#### F-078 · Stored XSS

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/userProfile.ts:73`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** The username is string-replaced into the Pug source with only a leading backslash, so raw HTML such as `<script>` may be rendered. The user's `profileImage`, which is stored raw whenever the fetch fails (`profileImageUrlUpload.ts:36`), is concatenated into the `Content-Security-Policy` header.

A value containing ; script-src 'unsafe-inline' therefore weakens the CSP.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-220](#m-220) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (🟡 [F-078](#f-078))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-080"></a><a id="f-080"></a>
#### F-080 · Cookie-authenticated profile update

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Juice Shop Express Server  ·  **Location:** `routes/updateUserProfile.ts:38`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-027](#w-027) - Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

**Issue:** POST `/profile` authenticates only with the token cookie and changes the username with no CSRF token or Origin check. A page on another site can auto-submit a form to change the victim's username, and the payload can carry the code-injection username from finding 002.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◑ [M-222](#m-222) — Require CSRF token and SameSite=Strict cookie

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-088"></a><a id="f-088"></a>
#### F-088 · Stored XSS

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** At `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`, LastLoginIpComponent decodes the JWT and passes `payload.data.lastLoginIp` into `DomSanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml`. The value is wrapped in a &lt;small&gt; element, marked trusted and bound to `innerHTML`.

The other part did not examine where the server gets the stored IP.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `last-login-ip.component.ts:39` calls `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` on a JWT claim, and the template binds it with [`innerHTML`] at line 10. The server stores the raw header at `saveLoginIp.ts:18-32`. The source does not show how a victim's own request would carry an attacker-chosen header, so the payload is shown reaching only the attacker's own account.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-230](#m-230) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (🟡 [F-088](#f-088))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-089"></a><a id="f-089"></a>
#### F-089 · Product descriptions marked as trusted HTML

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** At `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`, `trustProductDescription` uses `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` to mark every product description returned by the API as trusted HTML. If an attacker can store HTML in a product description, any view that binds the description with `innerHTML` runs it without Angular sanitization.

Neither part examined write access to product data.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-231](#m-231) — Stop trusting server product descriptions as HTML

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-090"></a><a id="f-090"></a>
#### F-090 · Chatbot auto-sends attacker-supplied

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/chatbot/chat-conversation/chat-conversation.component.ts:80`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-027](#w-027) - Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link to #`/chatbot/conversation/``&lt;id&gt;`?`initialMessage`=`&lt;prompt&gt;`. `ngOnInit` reads the query parameter and immediately calls `sendMessage`, which posts it to `/rest/chat` with the victim's bearer token added by the request interceptor.

The attacker thus injects a prompt into the victim's authenticated LLM session without user confirmation; if server-side chat tools act on the user's account, actions run on the victim's authority.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `chat-conversation.component.ts:77` reads `queryParams.initialMessage` and :80 calls `sendMessage` automatically; `chat.service.ts:37` posts the messages to `/rest/chat`; `request.interceptor.ts:16` attaches the `localStorage` bearer token. Server-side tool capabilities were not read, so concrete action impact is unproven.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◑ [M-232](#m-232) — Require explicit user confirmation before sending URL messages

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-072"></a><a id="f-072"></a>
#### F-072 · Missing Container Image Signing

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: The docker build-push step (lines 337-349) has no signing, but `release.yml` and other searched files were not served, so the absence is not fully established.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** Missing Container Image Signing: Unsigned container images cannot be verified for provenance - any registry intermediary could substitute them.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◑ [M-140](#m-140) — Sign release container images and publish attestations · ◑ [M-152](#m-152) — Manual review: verify Missing Container Image Signing

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-04](#tc-supply-04) · Rules: IAC-040

<a id="t-073"></a><a id="f-073"></a>
#### F-073 · Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-029](#w-029) - Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile`: 4; `package.json`: 56

**Issue:** Dependency Install Scripts Run at Build Time: Without `--ignore-scripts`, any compromised dependency publisher can execute postinstall code at build time.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-141](#m-141) — Install with npm ci --ignore-scripts in `Dockerfile` build

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-03](#tc-supply-03) · Rules: IAC-005

<a id="t-074"></a><a id="f-074"></a>
#### F-074 · Third-party GitHub Actions referenced by mutable branch and tags

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-5  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-048](#w-048) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-5](#tb-5) 🌐 External - external → CI/CD Pipeline · Authentication: The SHA-pinning assumption of tb-5 fails at this uses: reference, which resolves a mutable tag into CI.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-5 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Instances (3):** `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml`: 33; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: 188; `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml`: 23

**Issue:** Several workflows reference actions by a mutable branch or major tag instead of a commit SHA. Whoever controls the upstream ref, for example a compromised maintainer or a re-tag, can inject code into a tag that is already in use.

A moved v3 tag would run with `security-events: write` and could suppress or falsify code scanning results, although the workflow otherwise has a least-privilege permissions block.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◕ [M-217](#m-217) — Pin image workflow actions to full commit SHAs

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-01](#tc-supply-01) · Rules: IAC-011

<a id="t-075"></a><a id="f-075"></a>
#### F-075 · Lockfile generation disabled causing non-deterministic dependency

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.npmrc:1`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-048](#w-048) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Issue:** Because `.npmrc` disables package-lock, every npm install in CI, release and Docker builds resolves caret ranges fresh against the registry. An attacker who publishes a malicious version of any direct or transitive dependency within range (account takeover, typosquat in a transitive tree) gets it into release archives and Docker Hub images with no diff signal.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `.npmrc` line 1 disables lockfiles; `release.yml` line 55 and `Dockerfile` line 5 run npm install against semver ranges in `package.json`.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-218](#m-218) — Commit lockfiles and install from them

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-076"></a><a id="f-076"></a>
#### F-076 · Container base images referenced by mutable tag without digest

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-028](#w-028) - Container base images referenced by mutable tag without digest (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile`: 1; `test/smoke/Dockerfile`: 1

**Issue:** The `Dockerfile` builds from node:24 and gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13 by tag only, without a digest. `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1` uses alpine latest, also without a digest. In place: the images come from official and distroless publishers, and the runtime runs as non-root UID 65532.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◑ [M-163](#m-163) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-01](#tc-supply-01) · Rules: IAC-001

<a id="t-128"></a><a id="f-128"></a>
#### F-128 · Container Runs as Root

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Container Runs as Root: Containers running as root expand container-escape blast radius.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-142](#m-142) — Run smoke test container as a non-root user

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INFRA-01](#tc-infra-01) · Rules: IAC-002

<a id="t-129"></a><a id="f-129"></a>
#### F-129 · Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (13)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-025](#w-025) - Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Instances (13):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: 25; `.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml`: 13; `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml`: 21; `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml`: 6; `.github/workflows/rebase.yml`: 8; `.github/workflows/release.yml`: 10; `.github/workflows/stale.yml`: 7; `.github/workflows/update-challenges-ebook.yml`: 11 … (+5 more)

**Issue:** Missing Workflow Permissions Block: Without an explicit permissions block at workflow or job level, the job inherits the repository default (commonly write-all) for `GITHUB_TOKEN` - any compromised step can push code, create releases, or approve PRs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-143](#m-143) — Set least-privilege GITHUB_TOKEN permissions in `ci.yml`

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-CI-01](#tc-ci-01) · Rules: IAC-010

<a id="t-130"></a><a id="f-130"></a>
#### F-130 · Dependency lifecycle scripts run in secret-bearing release

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `package.json:56`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: The postinstall hook is confirmed, but workflow files showing secret-bearing release/test jobs running npm install without `--ignore-scripts` were not supplied.

**Weakness:** [W-048](#w-048) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Issue:** The root postinstall script runs a nested npm install and build, and test, e2e, smoke and release jobs run npm install without `--ignore-scripts`. With no lockfile, any newly published malicious dependency version executes install hooks in jobs that later hold `GITHUB_TOKEN` for release uploads, `ALCHEMY_API_KEY` and `CYPRESS_RECORD_KEY`, and can tamper with dist archives attached to releases.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-160](#m-160) — Manual review: verify Dependency lifecycle scripts run in secret-bearing release… · ◑ [M-270](#m-270) — Isolate install scripts from privileged release steps

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

_**Reliability** says how sure this report is that a finding is real, not how severe it is. It is a judgement, not a probability._

<tr><td style="overflow-wrap:break-word"><a id="ac-b-006"></a>AC-B-006 · User keeps using a withdrawn right</td><td style="overflow-wrap:break-word">No matched finding</td><td style="overflow-wrap:break-word">? _⚠ Fully viable: no effective control blocks the chain · ◐ Partially blocked: at least one step has a compensating control · ✓ Mitigated: the chain is broken at a verified step · ? Inconclusive: could not be verified end to end. Steps: ✓ confirmed · ✗ refuted · ? inconclusive._

_Proposed fixes address confirmed steps; their effectiveness still requires validation._

<a id="ac-t-003"></a>
### AC-T-003 — Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT Algorithm Confusion

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge an admin-role JWT without knowledge of the signing secret.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can obtain any valid JWT issued by the system (e.g. by registering a free account).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist | Verifier accepts attacker-chosen `alg` (e.g.<br/>`none` or HMAC-with-public-key), allowing<br/>token re-signing without the secret. |
| 2 | _no matching finding_<br/>`lib/insecurity.ts:172` | Forged `role: admin` claim is accepted as<br/>authoritative because the role is not<br/>re-fetched from the database per request. |

**Scenario impact**

Algorithm confusion alone yields a forgeable token; trusting the in-token role claim turns that forgery into instant admin access - neither gap is Critical in isolation, but the chain is a full authentication bypass.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-136](#m-136) — Pin allowed JWT algorithms on verification (**P1**): addresses 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-005"></a>
### AC-T-005 — Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge trusted tokens / credentials and impersonate any user.

**Prerequisite:** Signing material or other secrets are reachable (committed to a public repo, served by an unauthenticated route, or in an exposed directory).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Hardcoded JWT signing private key and HMAC keys in source | A private key, signing secret, or credential<br/>file is committed to the source repository<br/>or served without authentication. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist | The exposed key/secret is the same one the<br/>server trusts, so a token signed with it (or<br/>the leaked credential) is accepted as<br/>authentic. |

**Scenario impact**

Exposed signing material collapses the entire authentication boundary: any attacker who reads the key can mint a valid token for any identity or role, with no credential ever required.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-171](#m-171) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (🟠 [F-015](#f-015)) (**P1**): addresses 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Hardcoded JWT signing private key and HMAC keys in source at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.
- ● [M-136](#m-136) — Pin allowed JWT algorithms on verification (**P1**): addresses 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist at **Step 2**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

<a id="ac-t-004"></a>
### AC-T-004 — Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Obtain an administrator account without any existing privilege.

**Prerequisite:** Self-registration is open (one unauthenticated POST).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Mass assignment of role during user registration | The account-creation handler persists the<br/>request body wholesale, so a client-supplied<br/>`role` (or `isAdmin`) field is written verbatim. |

**Scenario impact**

A single unauthenticated request mints an admin account when the registration handler trusts a client-supplied role field - the most direct full-compromise path in role-based apps with open sign-up.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-205](#m-205) — Allowlist registration fields and force customer role (**P1**): addresses 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Mass assignment of role during user registration at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-007"></a>
### AC-T-007 — Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into a Tool-Calling Model

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Make the model invoke a privileged tool or return data the attacker is not entitled to, using the application's own authority.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can place text into a conversation, document, or record that the application later sends to a language model.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-031](#f-031) — Client-supplied chat history passed to model without role | The handler builds the prompt by<br/>interpolating request or stored text into<br/>the same string as the system instructions,<br/>so attacker text is read as instruction. |
| 2 | _no matching finding_<br/>`routes/chat.ts:207` | Tools exposed to the model run with the<br/>application's authority; a manipulated model<br/>turn therefore performs actions the caller<br/>could not. |

**Scenario impact**

Text an attacker can place anywhere the application later feeds to the model becomes instruction, and the tools behind that model run with the application's authority rather than the caller's - so a comment or a stored record reaches actions the attacker was never entitled to.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-180](#m-180) — Validate chat messages to user and assistant text only (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-031](#f-031) — Client-supplied chat history passed to model without role at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

<a id="ac-t-002"></a>
### AC-T-002 — Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - authenticated low-privilege user · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ◐ Partially blocked

**Goal:** Enumerate and exfiltrate other users' records, then escalate own permissions via unguarded mass assignment.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker holds a valid, non-privileged user account.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — IDOR | Attacker enumerates and retrieves records<br/>for arbitrary object IDs; no ownership<br/>comparison is performed. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Mass assignment of role during user registration | Update endpoint persists an unfiltered `role`<br/>(or equivalent) field supplied in the<br/>request body. |

**Scenario impact**

The ownership gap exposes every record, and the mass-assignment gap lets the same low-privilege actor self-elevate - together they turn a single compromised account into full tenant data access and role escalation.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-139](#m-139) — Scope basket item lookups to the caller (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — IDOR at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-006"></a>
### AC-T-006 — Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ◐ Partially blocked

**Goal:** Execute arbitrary code in the application process.

**Prerequisite:** An input reaches a server-side interpreter / template / eval.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-050](#f-050) — B2B order lines evaluated as code in vm and notevil | Attacker-controlled input is passed to `eval`,<br/>a server-side template engine, an unsafe<br/>sandbox, or an unsafe deserializer. |

**Scenario impact**

A single injection into a server-side interpreter yields code execution in the application process - the highest-impact outcome, granting full filesystem and network access from one unauthenticated request.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-199](#m-199) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (🟠 [F-050](#f-050)) (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-050](#f-050) — B2B order lines evaluated as code in vm and notevil at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.
