## 10. Mitigation Register

Changes are ordered by priority: P1 before deployment, P2 this sprint, P3 next quarter, P4 backlog. Each entry links its findings and gives the available implementation steps and checks.

### P1 — Immediate

<a id="m-136"></a>
#### M-136 — Pin allowed JWT algorithms on verification

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/verify.ts:120`

**How:**

1. Pass an explicit algorithms allowlist matching the signing key type to the verify call.
2. Reject tokens whose header alg is not in the allowlist, including none.

**Verification:** Submit a token with alg none and an `HS256` token signed with the public key; both must be rejected, while a valid `RS256` token is accepted.

---

<a id="m-144"></a>
#### M-144 — Manual review: verify Broken authorization attacker-controlled owner ID in resource query…

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Broken authorization

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/dataExport.ts:26`

**How:** The architect review left the assessment open: Evidence is ambiguous and no source shows the owner field reaching a query; Critical impact is unproven. Fix was missing; added a general one. The evidence-verifier sample could not confirm or refute the claim from the cited snippet alone. Have a developer familiar with this code path read ±20 lines around the cited location (`routes/dataExport.ts:26`) and decide whether to keep, downgrade, or remove this finding. Confirm the finding before implementing its fix (◕ [M-135](#m-135) — Derive record owner from the session, not the request body).

---

<a id="m-166"></a>
#### M-166 — Use parameterized replacements for search criteria

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-003](#f-003) — SQL injection in product search query

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**How:**

1. Replace the template string with `sequelize.query` using replacements, for example name LIKE :q with replacements: { q: `%${criteria}%` }.
2. Alternatively, use `ProductModel.findAll` with `Op.like` on name and description.
3. Validate that q is a string, and reject arrays or objects.

**Verification:** Run curl `"http://localhost:3000/rest/products/search?q=qwert'))%20UNION%20SELECT%20id,email,password,'4','5','6','7','8','9'%20FROM%20Users--"` and confirm that the response contains no user rows and is an empty or normal product list.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-167"></a>
#### M-167 — Use parameterized queries for login lookup

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — SQL injection in login credential query allows

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Replace the raw query with `UserModel.findOne({ where: { email: req.body.email, password: security.hash(req.body.password), deletedAt: null } })`, or use `sequelize.query` with replacements or bind parameters.
2. Before the query runs, reject email and password values that are not strings or are malformed.

**Verification:** Send POST `/rest/user/login` with email "admin@juice-`sh.op`'--" and a wrong password, and assert that the response is 401 with no token.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: SQL Injection Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/SQL_Injection_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-168"></a>
#### M-168 — Remove eval and pass username as Pug locals

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Server-side code execution from stored username

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**How:**

1. Delete the eval branch and treat username content only as text.
2. Stop string-replacing user data into Pug source; compile the static template once and pass username as a local so Pug escapes it with #{username}.
3. Validate usernames on write against a strict character allowlist.

**Verification:** Set the username to #{7*7}, to #{1+1} and to a `child_process` payload, and confirm GET `/profile` shows the literal text, never 49 or 2, and that no command runs.

**Reference:** [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html)

---

<a id="m-171"></a>
#### M-171 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (F-015)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Hardcoded JWT signing private key and HMAC keys in source

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**How:**

1. Remove the private key literal at line 21 and the HMAC key literals, including the one at line 42.
2. Load the keys at startup from environment-injected secrets, a secret manager or a KMS.
3. Rotate the RSA key pair and the HMAC keys, invalidate the tokens already issued, and re-hash the stored security answers.

**Verification:** Grep the source tree for 'PRIVATE KEY' and HMAC key literals and expect no match. Sign a token with the old key and confirm that a protected route rejects it with 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-321: Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)

---

<a id="m-204"></a>
#### M-204 — Strip role and privileged fields from registration input

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-059](#f-059) — User registration accepts a client-supplied admin role

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:408`

**How:**

1. In the POST `/api/Users` pre-handler, delete `req.body.role`, `deluxeToken`, `isActive`, `profileImage` and `totpSecret` before finale runs.
2. Configure the finale User create endpoint with an explicit attribute allow-list (email, password, username, `securityQuestion`).
3. Assign roles only through an admin-only, role-checked endpoint.

**Verification:** curl -X POST `/api/Users` -H '`Content-Type`: application/json' -d '{"email":"t@`x.io`","password":"p","passwordRepeat":"p","role":"admin"}' should return a user whose role is 'customer'.

---

<a id="m-205"></a>
#### M-205 — Allowlist registration fields and force customer role

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Mass assignment of role during user registration

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:484`

**How:**

1. In a finale `create.write.before` hook or a middleware on POST `/api/Users`, delete role, `deluxeToken`, `isActive`, `profileImage`, `totpSecret` and id from `req.body`, or rebuild the body from an allow-list of the email, password and `securityQuestion` fields.
2. Set role to customer on the server for every self-registration.
3. Allow privileged roles only through an endpoint that requires admin authorization.

**Verification:** Send an unauthenticated POST `/api/Users` with `{"email":"t@x.y","password":"Passw0rd!","passwordRepeat":"Passw0rd!","role":"admin"}`. Assert that the response `data.role` is customer and that the stored user's role is customer.

**Reference:** [CWE-915: Improperly Controlled Modification of Dynamically-Determined Object Attributes (Mass Assignment)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

---

<a id="m-206"></a>
#### M-206 — Allow-list registration fields and force the customer role

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-061](#f-061) — Registration lets the request body set the admin role

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Add a `create.write.before` hook on the User resource that deletes role, `deluxeToken`, `isActive` and `profileImage` from `req.body`.
2. Alternatively, replace the generated create endpoint with a custom handler that assigns only email, password and security answers.

**Verification:** Run POST `/api/Users` with role set to admin, then GET the created user with an admin token and assert that role equals customer.

---

<a id="m-283"></a>
#### M-283 — Bind security answers to the authenticated user

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-143](#f-143) — Security answer creation accepts owner UserId from request body

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Create security answers only inside registration, or require authentication and set UserId on the server, for example with `appendUserId`.
2. Exclude UserId from client-writable attributes of the generated create route.

**Verification:** POST `/api/SecurityAnswers` without a token, or with a UserId different from the caller's, is rejected and creates no row.

---

### P2 — This Sprint

<a id="m-135"></a>
#### M-135 — Derive record owner from the session, not the request body

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Broken authorization

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/dataExport.ts:26`

**How:**

1. Ignore any owner id supplied in the request body and take the user id from the verified session identity.
2. Use that session id in every query filter for the user's records.
3. Return 403 or 404 when no record owned by the caller matches.

**Verification:** As user A, send a request naming user B's id as owner and confirm only A's data is returned or the request is rejected; A's own requests still work.

---

<a id="m-137"></a>
#### M-137 — Use a CSPRNG for security tokens and secrets

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-047](#w-047)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-330](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/330.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:53`

**How:**

1. Replace `Math.random` or other non-cryptographic generators used for secret values with `crypto.randomBytes` or `crypto.randomUUID`.
2. Review other token, reset-link and OTP generation sites for the same generator and replace them.

**Verification:** Grep the source for `Math.random` in security-value code and expect no match. Generate 1000 tokens and confirm they are unique and unpredictable, and that login still works.

---

<a id="m-138"></a>
#### M-138 — Replace security-answer reset with emailed one-time token

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-021](#f-021) — Password reset accepts a security-question answer

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:10`

**How:**

1. Issue a CSPRNG reset token, single-use and short-lived, delivered to the account's verified email.
2. Require the token on `/rest/user/reset-password` and remove the security-answer check as the sole proof.
3. Invalidate the token after use and rate limit attempts per account.

**Verification:** Post a reset with a guessed security answer and no token and expect rejection. Complete a reset with a valid emailed token and confirm it works once, then fails on reuse.

<a id="m-139"></a>
#### M-139 — Scope basket item lookups to the caller

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — IDOR

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/basketItems.ts:68`

**How:**

1. Add the authenticated user's basket or user id to the query filter for the item.
2. Return 403 or 404 when the item is not owned by the caller.

**Verification:** As user A, request user B's item id and confirm 403 or 404 with B's data unchanged; A's own items still work.

<a id="m-165"></a>
#### M-165 — Scope basket lookup to the authenticated user

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing ownership checks on basket endpoints allow cross-user basket access

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:426`

**How:**

1. In the basket retrieval, coupon and checkout handlers, look up the basket with where: { id, UserId: `req.body.UserId` }, using the UserId that `appendUserId` sets.
2. Return 403 or 404 when no basket owned by the caller matches.
3. In PUT `/api/BasketItems/:id`, load the item with its BasketId and return 403 unless it equals the caller's bid.
4. Strip BasketId and ProductId from the basket item update body.
5. Add tests for cross-user basket reads, item updates, coupon application and checkout.

**Verification:** As user A, send each of these requests against user B's basket and expect 403 or 404 every time, with B's basket, items, quantities and coupon unchanged: GET `/rest/basket/`&lt;B's id>, PUT `/api/BasketItems/`&lt;item in B's basket> with quantity 5, PUT `/rest/basket/`&lt;B's id>`/coupon/`&lt;coupon&gt;, and POST `/rest/basket/`&lt;B's id>`/checkout`.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

<a id="m-169"></a>
#### M-169 — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat identity

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-012](#f-012) — Chatbot order lookup trusts identity from an unverified JWT

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:45`

**How:**

1. In `getUserId`, replace `security.decode` with `security.verify(token)` or `jwt.verify` against the public key with algorithms ['RS256'], and decode only after verification succeeds.
2. Treat a verification failure as unauthenticated, or resolve the user through `security.authenticatedUsers.from(req)`.
3. Register `security.isAuthorized()` on POST `/rest/chat` wherever tools that touch user data are enabled.
4. Check order ownership by an immutable user id or the unmasked email instead of comparing masked emails.

**Verification:** Send POST `/rest/chat` with an unsigned or altered JWT that contains another user's id, and ask `getOrderById` for that user's order. Expect 'Customer not authenticated' and no order data.

**Reference:** [CWE-347: Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html)

---

<a id="m-170"></a>
#### M-170 — Exchange OAuth tokens server-side instead of derived passwords

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-014](#f-014) — OAuth account password predictably derived

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Remove the client-side password derivation from `oauth.component.ts`.
2. Send the OAuth credential to a server endpoint that verifies the provider token for this client before it issues the session.
3. Create OAuth-linked accounts on the server with a random or unusable password, and reject password logins for them.
4. Reset or rotate the derived passwords of existing OAuth-created accounts.

**Verification:** For an OAuth-created account, POST `/rest/user/login` with the email and password `btoa(reversed email)`. Confirm the server rejects it with 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

<a id="m-172"></a>
#### M-172 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (F-016)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — JWT signing private key is embedded in source code

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:54`

**How:**

1. Remove the PEM literal and load the private key from a secret manager or a protected file at startup.
2. Rotate the key pair so that tokens signed with the leaked key fail verification.
3. Also stop deriving `deluxeToken` from the same key.

**Verification:** After rotation, sign a token with the old embedded key and assert that GET `/rest/2fa/status` returns 401.

<a id="m-173"></a>
#### M-173 — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — JWT verification without a pinned signature algorithm

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**How:**

1. Replace `jws.verify(token, publicKey)` with `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })` in `verify()`.
2. Configure `expressJwt` with algorithms: ['RS256'] in `isAuthorized()` and upgrade `express-jwt` and jws to current versions.

**Verification:** Send a token with header alg none, then one `HS256`-signed with the public key, to `/rest/order-history/orders`. Both must return 403/401.

**Reference:** [CWE-347: Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html)

---

<a id="m-174"></a>
#### M-174 — Replace answer-based reset with emailed one-time tokens

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — Password reset by security answer with spoofable

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Send a single-use, short-lived reset token to the account email and require it to reset the password.
2. Key the rate limit on the account email and the true client IP, and disable trust proxy unless a known proxy is in front.
3. Lock or delay reset attempts per account after repeated failures.

**Verification:** Send 150 reset requests for one email with a different X-Forwarded-For each time and assert that responses after the limit are 429.

- 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — Request-chosen layout path allows file access

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/dataErasure.ts:104`

**How:**

2. Pass only expected fields to `res.render` instead of spreading `req.body`.

<a id="m-176"></a>
#### M-176 — Confine zip entries to the extraction directory

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — Zip slip in file upload allows writes anywhere under

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Resolve each entry against `path.resolve('uploads/complaints')`, and reject it unless the result starts with that directory plus the path separator.
2. Write to the resolved, validated path.
3. Reject entries that have absolute paths or contain '..' segments.
4. Cap the total decompressed size and the number of entries.
5. Make `checkFileType` and `checkUploadSize` reject disallowed file types and oversized files.

**Verification:** Upload a zip containing the entry ../..`/ftp/legal.md` to `/file-upload`. Confirm that the request is rejected and that `ftp/legal.md` is unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-22: Path Traversal](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

<a id="m-177"></a>
#### M-177 — Verify token signatures before trusting claims

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — Coupons accepted without integrity protection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/coupon.ts:15`

**How:**

1. Store issued coupons server-side with their discount, expiry and single-use state, or sign them with an HMAC over a secret key and verify it.
2. Cap the accepted discount at the business maximum during validation.

**Verification:** Apply a self-encoded `z85` coupon 'MMMYY-99' for the current month and expect 'Invalid coupon.'

<a id="m-178"></a>
#### M-178 — Use typed query predicates instead of \$where

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — NoSQL \$where JavaScript injection in product reviews and order tracking

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-009](#w-009)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`

**How:**

1. Always coerce the product id with `Number()` and reject NaN.
2. Query reviews with { product: id } instead of a \$where string.
3. Remove `global.sleep`.
4. Always apply the [\w-] allowlist to the order id.
5. Query orders with `ordersCollection.find({ orderId: id })` instead of \$where.
6. Return only delivery-status fields to anonymous order-tracking callers.

**Verification:** Request `/rest/products/sleep`(2000)`/reviews` and `/rest/products/1%20`||%20true/reviews and confirm a fast 400 or only product 1's reviews, then request `/rest/track-order/`'%20||%20true%20||%20' and confirm at most one order is returned.

**Reference:** [CWE-943: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic (NoSQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html)

<a id="m-179"></a>
#### M-179 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-030)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Stored username compiled into Pug template source

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-019](#w-019)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1336](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:87`

**How:**

1. Keep views/`userProfile.pug` static and reference #{username} in the template itself instead of substituting the username into the template source.
2. Call `fn({ username, ...user })` so the value is rendered as escaped data and never compiled.

**Verification:** Set the username to a Pug code line such as "- var x = 7*7" followed by #{x}, request `/profile`, and confirm the page shows the literal text and not 49.

<a id="m-181"></a>
#### M-181 — Stop trusting feedback HTML and use text bindings

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-032](#f-032) — Stored XSS from feedback comments on the About page

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`

**How:**

1. Remove the `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` call and render the comment with text interpolation, keeping only the static star markup as template HTML.
2. If rich text is required, sanitize the comment with `DomSanitizer.sanitize(SecurityContext.HTML)` or an allowlist sanitizer before display.
3. Sanitize feedback comments on the server before storing them.
4. Add a strict `Content-Security-Policy` without unsafe-inline to limit the impact of any remaining injection.

**Verification:** POST `/api/Feedbacks` with comment '&lt;img `src=x` `onerror=alert`(1)&gt;', then load /#`/about` in Cypress. Assert that no alert fires and that the markup appears as escaped text.

**Reference:** [CWE-79: Improper Neutralization of Input During Web Page Generation (XSS)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)

<a id="m-184"></a>
#### M-184 — Remove sanitizer bypass and render feedback as text

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-035](#f-035) — Stored XSS

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:91`

4. Sanitize or encode feedback comments on the server when they are stored.
5. Add a strict `Content-Security-Policy` without unsafe-inline.

**Verification:** Run a Cypress e2e test that POSTs `/api/Feedbacks` with comment '&lt;img `src=x` `onerror=window.__xss`=1&gt;', then opens #`/administration` and the feedback details dialog as admin. Assert that `window.__xss` is undefined, no alert fires, and the text is shown literally.

<a id="m-192"></a>
#### M-192 — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution (F-043)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-043](#f-043) — XML parser enables external entity and DTD loading

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-023](#w-023)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) - XML External Entity (XXE)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/xml.ts:35`

**How:**

1. Remove `XML_PARSE_NOENT` and `XML_PARSE_DTDLOAD` from the parse options and stop calling `xmlRegisterFsInputProviders`.
2. Reject documents that contain a DOCTYPE before parsing.

**Verification:** Parse an XML document that declares an entity pointing to `file:///etc/passwd` and assert that the output does not contain passwd content.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: XML External Entity Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/XML_External_Entity_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-193"></a>
#### M-193 — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution (F-044)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-044](#f-044) — XML parser resolves external entities from host filesystem

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) - XML External Entity (XXE)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/xml.ts:38`

**How:**

1. Remove `XML_PARSE_NOENT` and `XML_PARSE_DTDLOAD` from the parse options.
2. Do not register filesystem input providers (`xmlRegisterFsInputProviders`).

**Verification:** Upload an XML file with &lt;!ENTITY x SYSTEM "file:///etc/passwd"> to `/file-upload` and confirm that the response does not contain passwd entries.

---

<a id="m-194"></a>
#### M-194 — Validate and allowlist outbound request targets

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-045](#f-045) — Server-side request forgery

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) - Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`

**How:**

1. Parse `imageUrl` and allow only the https scheme.
2. Allow only hostnames from a configured allowlist of image hosts.
3. Resolve the host via DNS and reject private, loopback, link-local and metadata address ranges.
4. Disable redirects with redirect: 'manual', or re-validate each redirect target.
5. Limit the response size and verify that the content type is an image before writing the file.

**Verification:** With a session cookie, POST `/profile/image/url` with `imageUrl` set to `http://169.254.169.254/`, `http://127.0.0.1:3000/metrics` and `http://127.0.0.1:3000/rest/admin/application-configuration`. Confirm that each request is rejected with an error and that no outbound request is made.

**Reference:** [CWE-918: Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

---

<a id="m-195"></a>
#### M-195 — Return a minimal status from password reset

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-046](#f-046) — Password reset response leaks password hash

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-031](#w-031)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) - Exposure of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:46`

**How:**

1. Respond with a status message only, never the updated user model.
2. Leave 2FA secrets out of every serialized User response.

**Verification:** POST a valid reset for a user with 2FA enabled and assert that the response body has no `totpSecret` or password field.

---

<a id="m-196"></a>
#### M-196 — Return only a status from password reset

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-047](#f-047) — Password reset response leaks hash and TOTP secret

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-201](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/201.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:46`

**How:**

1. Replace `res.json({ user: updatedUser })` with a minimal success status and no user fields.
2. Replace security-question recovery with a single-use emailed reset token, and require the second factor when `totpSecret` is set.

**Verification:** Reset a 2FA-enabled user's password via POST `/rest/user/reset-password` and confirm the response contains no password or `totpSecret`.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Forgot Password Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Forgot_Password_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-197"></a>
#### M-197 — Hash passwords with bcrypt or argon2id

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-048](#f-048) — Passwords stored as unsalted MD5 hashes

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-047](#w-047)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Replace `security.hash` in the User password setter with argon2id, scrypt or bcrypt (cost 12 or higher) using a per-user salt.
2. Verify passwords with the library's verify function in login, 2FA setup, 2FA disable and change-password instead of the SQL string match.
3. Rehash each legacy `MD5` value at the user's next successful login, and force a password reset for accounts that are not migrated.
4. Add a minimum length and strength check, for example 12 or more characters plus a breached-password check, to registration, password change and reset.

**Verification:** Create two users with the same password, then run `sqlite3` data/juiceshop.sqlite `"select password from Users order by id desc limit 2"`. Confirm both values start with \$argon2id\$ or \$2b\$, differ from each other and are not 32 hex characters. Confirm that creating a user with the password '123' is rejected.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Password Storage Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Password_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-198"></a>
#### M-198 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (F-049)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-049](#f-049) — Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-016](#w-016)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:203`

**How:**

1. Apply `express-rate-limit` per user/IP on `/rest/chat`.
2. Set `maxOutputTokens` and cap message count and total input length.
3. Alert on token usage metrics.

**Verification:** Send 200 rapid requests to `/rest/chat`; expect 429 after the configured threshold.

**Reference:** [OWASP GenAI: LLM102025 Unbounded Consumption](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm102025-unbounded-consumption/)

<a id="m-199"></a>
#### M-199 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-050)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-050](#f-050) — B2B order lines evaluated as code in vm and notevil

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`

**How:**

1. Remove the vm and notevil `safeEval` path entirely and parse `orderLinesData` with `JSON.parse`.
2. Validate the order-line structure against a schema.
3. Bound the body size and add a rate limit to the B2B endpoint.

**Verification:** POST `orderLinesData`=`"(function `dos()` { `while(true)`; })()"` to `/b2b/v2/orders` with a valid token and confirm an HTTP 400 response without any evaluation delay.

**Reference:** [CWE-94: Improper Control of Generation of Code (Code Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

<a id="m-200"></a>
#### M-200 — Require and verify current password on change

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-051](#f-051) — Password change skips current password check

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-014](#w-014)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**How:**

1. Return 401 when the current password is missing or does not match, using a proper password hash comparison.
2. Move the endpoint to POST with a JSON body so that passwords do not appear in URLs or logs.
3. Invalidate the user's other cached sessions after a password change.

**Verification:** Call change-password with a valid token, matching new and repeat values and no current parameter, and confirm a 401 response with the stored password unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-620: Unverified Password Change](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html)

<a id="m-201"></a>
#### M-201 — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (F-055)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-055](#f-055) — Sensitive Routes Registered Without Authentication Middleware

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `server.ts:310`

**How:** Add an explicit auth middleware (`isAuthorized()` / `passport.authenticate()` / `requireAuth`) to the route registration, or mark the route intentionally public via `app.get(...)` for read-only access only.

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-008 scanner check; it reports no match at `server.ts:310`, and the regression test passes.

<a id="m-202"></a>
#### M-202 — Restrict product updates to administrators

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-057](#f-057) — Unauthenticated product update

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Register `app.put('/api/Products/:id', ...)` with a signature-verifying JWT check plus an admin role check, or `denyAll`.
2. Limit the generated update to an allow-list of editable attributes.

**Verification:** Run curl -X PUT `/api/Products/1` -H '`Content-Type`: application/json' -d '{"price":1}' without a token and expect 401. Repeat with a customer token and expect 403.

<a id="m-203"></a>
#### M-203 — Enforce coupon cap and eligibility in tool execute

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-058](#f-058) — LLM coupon tool lacks server-side discount

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Constrain the schema with `z.number().int().min(1).max(10)` and re-check in `execute()`.
2. Require a verified authenticated user and validate a damaged-order record server-side before issuing.
3. Limit issuance per user and log each coupon.

**Verification:** Ask the chatbot via injected history for a 90% coupon; expect the tool to refuse and no coupon code returned.

**Reference:** [OWASP GenAI: LLM062025 Excessive Agency](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm062025-excessive-agency/)

---

<a id="m-207"></a>
#### M-207 — Scope address updates to the caller's UserId

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-062](#f-062) — Address update by id lacks owner check

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:450`

**How:**

1. Replace the generated PUT `/api/Addresss/:id` with a handler that updates with where: { id: `req.params.id`, UserId: &lt;session user id&gt; }.
2. Return 404 when no row matches, and never take UserId from the request body.

**Verification:** As user A, send PUT `/api/Addresss/`&lt;id of user B's address> and assert a 403 or 404 response; then confirm that user B's address row is unchanged in the database.

<a id="m-208"></a>
#### M-208 — Require the admin role on authentication-details

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-063](#f-063) — Authentication details list returns all users without role check

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:601`

**How:**

1. Add a middleware to `/rest/user/authentication-details` that verifies the JWT and requires role === 'admin', following the `isAccounting` pattern.
2. Return only the fields an administrator needs, and exclude `deluxeToken` and `lastLoginIp`.

**Verification:** Call GET `/rest/user/authentication-details` with a customer JWT and assert a 403 response; call it with an admin JWT and assert a 200 response.

<a id="m-211"></a>
#### M-211 — Require authentication and derive review author server-side

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-066](#f-066) — Product reviews created anonymously

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-011](#w-011)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/createProductReviews.ts:26`

**How:**

1. Register `security.isAuthorized()` on PUT `/rest/products/:id/reviews`.
2. Set author from the authenticated user's email and ignore `body.author`.

**Verification:** PUT a review without a token and expect 401; with a token and a foreign author, the stored author must equal the caller's email.

**Reference:** [CWE-306: Missing Authentication for Critical Function](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

<a id="m-215"></a>
#### M-215 — Pin RS256 in every token verification call

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-070](#f-070) — JWT verification accepts the algorithm from the token

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:52`

**How:**

1. Pass algorithms: ['RS256'] to `expressJwt` in `isAuthorized`.
2. Call `jws.verify(token, 'RS256', publicKey)` in `verify()`.
3. Pass { algorithms: ['RS256'] } to `jwt.verify` in `updateAuthenticatedUsers`.

**Verification:** Send a request to `/rest/2fa/status` with an `HS256` token signed using the public key, and another with alg none, and assert both return 401.

<a id="m-217"></a>
#### M-217 — Pin image workflow actions to full commit SHAs

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-074](#f-074) — Third-party GitHub Actions referenced by mutable branch and tags

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-048](#w-048)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33`

**How:**

1. Pin calibreapp/image-actions@main, peter-evans/create-pull-request@v8 and actions/checkout@v6 in `image_actions.yml` to full commit SHAs with version comments.
2. Add an explicit permissions block to `image_actions.yml` that grants only contents: write and `pull-requests: write`.
3. Pin coverallsapp/github-action@v2 at `ci.yml` line 188 to its full commit SHA with a version comment.
4. Pin github/codeql-action/init, autobuild and analyze in `codeql-analysis.yml` to the same full commit SHA with a version comment.
5. Keep the pinned SHAs updated with Dependabot or Renovate for the github-actions ecosystem.

**Verification:** Run grep -nE 'uses: [`^@]+@(main|v[0-9]+)\s*$`' `.github/workflows/*.yml` and confirm it returns no matches. Confirm that `image_actions.yml` line 33, `ci.yml` line 188 and `codeql-analysis.yml` lines 23, 34 and 36 each reference a 40-character SHA.

**Reference:** [GitHub Docs: Security Hardening For Github Actions](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-guides/security-hardening-for-github-actions#using-third-party-actions)

<a id="m-235"></a>
#### M-235 — Authenticate socket handshake and omit flags

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-011](#w-011)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Add `io.use()` middleware that verifies a session or JWT in `socket.handshake.auth` before accepting the connection.

<a id="m-140"></a>

4. Document a cosign verify command with the expected workflow identity for consumers.

**Verification:** Run cosign verify against the published image digest with the expected certificate identity and confirm success; confirm verification fails for an unsigned or re-tagged image. Confirm docker pull and run still work.

<a id="m-163"></a>
#### M-163 — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-076](#f-076) — Container base images referenced by mutable tag without digest

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-028](#w-028)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) - Use of Unmaintained Third-Party Components

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:1`

**How:** Pin base image to @sha256:`<digest>` - Tag-only base images (FROM node:24) can be silently substituted by a malicious publisher. Digest-pinning (@sha256:…) ensures the exact image bytes are used on every build.

1. Reference node:24@sha256:`<digest>` and gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13@sha256:`<digest>` on `Dockerfile` lines 1 and 22.
2. Pin the alpine image in `test/smoke/Dockerfile` by digest.
3. Let Renovate or Dependabot refresh the pinned digests.

**Verification:** Run grep -n '^FROM' `Dockerfile` `test/smoke/Dockerfile` and confirm that every FROM line in both files contains @sha256:.

**Reference:** [docs.docker.com: Best Practices](https://docs.docker.com/build/building/best-practices/#pin-base-image-versions)

---

<a id="m-164"></a>
#### M-164 — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — Session JWT stored in script-readable browser storage

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-010](#w-010)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) - Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** High · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`

**How:**

1. Have the server issue the session token in a `Set-Cookie` header with HttpOnly, Secure and `SameSite=Strict` or Lax, and stop returning the JWT to script.
2. Remove the `localStorage.setItem('token')` and client-side `cookieService.put('token')` calls from every login path.
3. Keep the 2FA temporary token in memory or in a server-set HttpOnly cookie instead of `localStorage`.
4. Add CSRF protection for requests authenticated by cookie.

**Verification:** After each login type, confirm in browser devtools that `localStorage` has no 'token' or 'totp_tmp_token' entry, that `document.cookie` does not contain the token, and that the token cookie shows HttpOnly and Secure.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: HTML5 Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/HTML5_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html#local-storage)

---

<a id="m-209"></a>
#### M-209 — Validate redirect targets against an allowlist

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-064](#f-064) — Open redirect

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/redirect.ts:19`

**How:**

1. Replace the `includes()` check with exact membership: `redirectAllowlist.has(toUrl)`.
2. Alternatively, map short IDs to allowlisted URLs and accept only the ID in the query parameter.

**Verification:** Request `/redirect?to`=`https://evil.example/?x=https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop` and expect 406. Request an exact allowlisted URL and expect a 302 to it.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Unvalidated Redirects And Forwards Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Unvalidated_Redirects_and_Forwards_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-210"></a>
#### M-210 — Verify challenge solutions server-side with authenticated sockets

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-065](#f-065) — Server trusts unauthenticated client-reported

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** High · **File:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Require authenticated socket connections before accepting verify* events.
2. Where feasible, detect solutions from server-observed behaviour instead of client-reported strings.

**Verification:** Emit `verifyCloseNotificationsChallenge` with [1,2] from an unauthenticated client and confirm the challenge stays unsolved.

**Reference:** [CWE-602: Client-Side Enforcement of Server-Side Security](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

<a id="m-214"></a>
#### M-214 — Use authorization code flow with PKCE and state

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-069](#f-069) — OAuth implicit flow without state, nonce or PKCE

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-026](#w-026)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) - Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**How:**

1. Replace `response_type=token` with `response_type=code` and use PKCE with an S256 `code_challenge` and `code_verifier`.
2. Generate a random state value, store it in `sessionStorage`, and reject callbacks whose state does not match.
3. Exchange the authorization code on the server.
4. Validate the nonce in the ID token on the server.

**Verification:** Open /#`access_token=foo` with no prior authorization request and no matching state, and confirm the app refuses to sign in. Confirm the authorization URL contains state and `code_challenge` parameters.

**Reference:** [datatracker.ietf.org: Rfc9700](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9700)

---

<a id="m-216"></a>
#### M-216 — Validate exp, iss and aud in the verify helper

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-071](#f-071) — Token verification helper never checks expiry

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**How:**

1. Replace `jws.verify` with `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'], issuer, audience })`.
2. Set issuer and audience in `authorize()` and give `tmpToken` and `setupToken` short expiries.

**Verification:** Call `/rest/2fa/verify` with an expired `tmpToken` and a valid TOTP code and assert a 401 response.

<a id="m-218"></a>
#### M-218 — Commit lockfiles and install from them

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-075](#f-075) — Lockfile generation disabled causing non-deterministic dependency

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-048](#w-048)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `.npmrc:1`

**How:**

1. Remove the package-lock setting from `.npmrc` and commit `package-lock.json` for root and frontend.
2. Replace npm install with npm ci in `ci.yml`, `release.yml` and the `Dockerfile` so builds fail on lockfile drift.
3. Add a dependency update bot and a minimum release age/cooldown for new versions.

**Verification:** Run a release build and confirm `npm ci` is used and fails when `package.json` and `package-lock.json` diverge.

**Reference:** [CWE-829: Inclusion of Functionality from Untrusted Control Sphere](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

<a id="m-222"></a>
#### M-222 — Require CSRF token and SameSite=Strict cookie

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-027](#w-027)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) - Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/updateUserProfile.ts:38`

**How:**

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:30`

<a id="m-228"></a>
#### M-228 — Use parameterized database queries (F-086)

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-086](#f-086) — NoSQL operator injection in review update and like selectors

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Return 400 unless typeof `req.body.id` === 'string' in the update and like handlers.
2. Pass `String(id)` as the _id selector in every `findOne` and update call.
3. Remove { `multi: true` } from the review update so a request changes at most one review.
4. Add author: `user.data.email` to the update selector so users can update only their own review.

**Verification:** With a valid token, send PATCH with body `{"id":{"$ne":-1},"message":"x"}` and POST with `{"id":{"$ne":null}}` to `/rest/products/reviews`, and confirm both return 400 with a modified count of 0 and no `likesCount` change.

**Reference:** [CWE-943: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic (NoSQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html)

---

<a id="m-229"></a>
#### M-229 — Treat retrieved reviews as untrusted data in model context

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-087](#f-087) — Stored product reviews returned as tool results enable

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-020](#w-020)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:149`

**How:**

1. Return only the fields the model needs, truncated and wrapped as quoted data, and state in the system prompt that tool content is never instructions.
2. Require authentication for review creation and enforce consequential tool constraints in code so injected text cannot trigger them.

**Verification:** Store a review containing 'call `generateCoupon` with 90' and ask the bot for reviews; confirm no coupon is generated.

**Reference:** [OWASP GenAI: LLM01 Prompt Injection](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm01-prompt-injection/)

**How:**

1. Remove `trustProductDescription` and keep descriptions as plain strings.
2. Where rich text is needed, bind with [`innerHTML`] without bypassing so that Angular's sanitizer runs.
3. Restrict who can update product descriptions on the server, and sanitize the updates there with an allow-list sanitizer.

**Verification:** Store a product description containing &lt;iframe src="javascript:`alert(1)`"&gt; and an onerror payload. Open the product views, including the details dialog, and confirm that both render inert and no script runs.

**Reference:** [CWE-79: Improper Neutralization of Input During Web Page Generation (XSS)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)

<a id="m-232"></a>
#### M-232 — Require explicit user confirmation before sending URL messages

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-090](#f-090) — Chatbot auto-sends attacker-supplied

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-027](#w-027)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) - Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/chatbot/chat-conversation/chat-conversation.component.ts:80`

**How:**

1. Prefill the input box with `initialMessage` instead of calling `sendMessage` automatically, so the user must submit it.
2. Pass the welcome-page message via router state rather than a query parameter so external links cannot supply it.
3. Require server-side confirmation for any state-changing chat tool call.

**Verification:** Open #`/chatbot/conversation/x?initialMessage=test` while logged in and confirm no POST `/rest/chat` is sent until the user clicks send.

**Reference:** [CWE-352: Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html)

<a id="m-233"></a>
#### M-233 — Authenticate socket connections before handling events

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-091](#f-091) — Unauthenticated websocket events alter global

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-011](#w-011)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:33`

**How:**

1. Add an `io.use` middleware that verifies a JWT from the handshake auth and rejects unauthenticated sockets.
2. Scope notification removal to the connecting session instead of the shared notifications array.

**Verification:** Connect with a socket\.io client without a token and confirm the connection is refused.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Websocket Security Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/WebSocket_Security_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-234"></a>
#### M-234 — Derive login IP from the trusted socket address

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-092](#f-092) — Client-controlled header recorded as last login IP

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-348](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/348.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/saveLoginIp.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Use `req.socket.remoteAddress`, or `req.ip` behind a configured trust-proxy list, instead of the True-Client-IP header.
2. Record the login IP server-side in the login handler instead of via a client-triggered GET.

**Verification:** Call `/rest/saveLoginIp` with True-Client-IP: `1.2.3.4` and confirm the stored `lastLoginIp` is the real socket address.

**Reference:** [CWE-348](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/348.html)

#### M-236 — Stop passing request body into render options

- 🟡 [F-094](#f-094) — Local file read

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-73](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/73.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/dataErasure.ts:104`

**How:**

1. Pass only explicit fields to `res.render`. Never spread `req.body`.
2. Drop the layout parameter, or map it to a fixed allowlist of server-defined layouts.

**Verification:** POST `/dataerasure` with `layout=`..`/package.json` and a valid token cookie. The response must not contain `package.json` content.

<a id="m-251"></a>
#### M-251 — Query orders by authenticated user id

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-109](#f-109) — Orders looked up by non-unique vowel-masked e-mail

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/dataExport.ts:33`

**How:**

1. Persist the owner UserId on each order document at checkout.
2. Query `ordersCollection`, including `ordersCollection.find` in the order history, by the authenticated user's id (`loggedInUser.data.id`) instead of the masked e-mail.
3. Remove the masked-email lookup key.

**Verification:** Register two accounts whose e-mail addresses differ only in their vowels, and place orders with each. Confirm that the data export and GET `/rest/order-history` for each account return only that account's own orders.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-111](#f-111) — Hardcoded cookie signing secret in server setup

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) - Use of Hard-coded Credentials

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:289`

**How:**

1. Read the cookie secret from an environment variable or a secret manager at startup.
2. Fail startup if the secret is unset.
3. Rotate the secret after removing it from source.

**Verification:** Grep `server.ts` for the literal `cookieParser` secret and confirm nothing is found, and confirm startup fails without the environment variable.

<a id="m-254"></a>
#### M-254 — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm (F-112)

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-112](#f-112) — Passwords verified and stored as unsalted fast

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-047](#w-047)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**How:**

1. Replace the unsalted digest with bcrypt (cost 12 or higher) or Argon2id in the password setter.
2. Use the library's constant-time verify function in login and change-password instead of comparing digests.
3. Rehash legacy digests on the next successful login and force a reset for the remaining accounts.

**Verification:** Create a user and check that the stored password has a bcrypt or Argon2id prefix and that login still works.

<a id="m-255"></a>
#### M-255 — Send password changes as a POST request body

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-113](#f-113) — Current and new passwords sent in a GET query string

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-041](#w-041)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:54`

**How:**

1. Change the client and the server route to POST or PUT with the passwords in a JSON body.
2. Make the server route reject credentials sent in the query string.
3. Require the current password on the server and add CSRF protection for the password change.
4. Purge or redact existing access logs that contain change-password query strings.

**Verification:** Change a password and confirm the request is a POST or PUT and that no password appears in any URL in the network log or the access log.

**Reference:** [CWE-598: Use of GET Request Method With Sensitive Query Strings](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html)

<a id="m-256"></a>
#### M-256 — Send the access token in the Authorization header

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-114](#f-114) — OAuth access token carried in URL fragment and query string

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-042](#w-042)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:68`

**How:**

1. Call the userinfo endpoint with an '`Authorization`: Bearer `<token>`' header instead of the `access_token` query parameter.
2. Verify the token on the server instead of calling userinfo from the browser.
3. Move to an authorization code flow with PKCE so that no token appears in the URL and the server handles the tokens.

**Verification:** Inspect the browser network log during an OAuth login and confirm that no request URL, including the request to `googleapis.com`, contains `access_token`.

**Reference:** [datatracker.ietf.org: Rfc6750](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6750#section-5.3)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `models/card.ts:38`

**How:**

1. Replace raw card numbers with a payment-provider token, or encrypt `cardNum` with a key held in a secret store.
2. Store only the last four digits for display.
3. Migrate the existing rows and drop the plaintext column.

**Verification:** Run `sqlite3 data/juiceshop.sqlite 'select cardNum from Cards'` and confirm that no 16-digit numbers are returned.

---

<a id="m-266"></a>
#### M-266 — Encrypt TOTP seeds with an externally held key

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-124](#f-124) — TOTP secrets stored in plaintext user column

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-036](#w-036)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `models/user.ts:112`

**How:**

1. Encrypt `totpSecret` with AES-GCM in a model setter and decrypt it in a getter, using a key loaded from a secret store.
2. Re-encrypt the existing seeds in a migration.

**Verification:** Enable 2FA for a test user and confirm that the stored `totpSecret` is ciphertext, not a base32 seed.

---

<a id="m-267"></a>
#### M-267 — Validate addresses and cap the connected-wallet set

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-125](#f-125) — Unbounded in-memory wallet set growth

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-017](#w-017)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/web3Wallet.ts:16`

**How:**

1. Reject a `walletAddress` that fails ethers `isAddress`, and normalise it with `getAddress` before storing it.
2. Cap `walletsConnected` in size and expire entries with a TTL, for example with an LRU cache.
3. Rate-limit `/rest/web3/walletExploitAddress` for each client.

**Verification:** Send 100k requests with distinct valid addresses and confirm the set stays at its configured cap. Confirm requests with invalid addresses get a 400 response and are not stored.

**Reference:** [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html)

---

<a id="m-268"></a>
#### M-268 — Bound parser resource consumption

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-126](#f-126) — Synchronous XML and YAML parsing blocks event loop

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-776](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/776.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:109`

**How:**

1. Parse in a worker thread with low time and memory limits, and disable alias and entity expansion.
2. Require authentication and rate-limit `/file-upload`.

**Verification:** Send 10 concurrent YAML bomb uploads. Other endpoints must keep responding within normal latency.

**Reference:** [CWE-776](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/776.html)

---

<a id="m-269"></a>
#### M-269 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (F-127)

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-127](#f-127) — No brute-force throttling on login and password reset

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-013](#w-013)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:32`

**How:**

1. Register `rateLimit` keyed on account plus client IP for `/rest/user/login` and `/rest/user/reset-password`.
2. Add progressive delay or temporary lockout after repeated failures per account.

**Verification:** Send 20 failed logins for one account in a minute and expect 429 responses after the configured threshold.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-270"></a>
#### M-270 — Isolate install scripts from privileged release steps

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-130](#f-130) — Dependency lifecycle scripts run in secret-bearing release

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-048](#w-048)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `package.json:56`

**How:**

1. Use npm ci `--ignore-scripts` in CI and run only the project's own required build steps explicitly.
2. Split release packaging so the job that runs dependency code has no write token, and upload artifacts from a separate job with contents: write.
3. Generate build provenance/attestations for release archives.

**Verification:** Release workflow logs show npm ci `--ignore-scripts` and the packaging job's token lacks contents: write.

**Reference:** [CWE-829: Inclusion of Functionality from Untrusted Control Sphere](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

---

<a id="m-271"></a>
#### M-271 — Enforce admin role or ownership on feedback deletion

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-131](#f-131) — Any authenticated user can delete any customer feedback

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-285](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/285.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:361`

**How:**

1. Add a middleware on DELETE `/api/Feedbacks/:id` that verifies the JWT and requires the admin role.
2. Alternatively, load the feedback and compare its UserId with the authenticated user id before deleting.

**Verification:** As customer A, send DELETE `/api/Feedbacks/`&lt;id of B's feedback> and expect 403. Confirm the record still exists.

---

<a id="m-272"></a>
#### M-272 — Restrict product creation to an admin role

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-132](#f-132) — Any authenticated user can create catalogue products

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-285](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/285.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:369`

**How:**

1. Replace `isAuthorized()` on POST `/api/Products` with a server-side check of an admin role from the verified token.
2. Apply the same guard to PUT `/api/Products/:id`.

**Verification:** Run an integration test: POST `/api/Products` with a customer token must return 401/403, and with an admin token 201.

---

<a id="m-273"></a>
#### M-273 — Derive BasketId from session and reject duplicate keys

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-133](#f-133) — Duplicate JSON keys bypass BasketId ownership check

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/basketItems.ts:37`

**How:**

1. Set BasketId from the authenticated user's bid instead of the request body, or reject bodies whose keys repeat.
2. Validate and store the same parsed quantity value that the stock and limit check used.

**Verification:** POST a raw body with two BasketId keys (own, then another user's) and expect HTTP 401 or 400, with no item created in the other basket.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

---

<a id="m-274"></a>
#### M-274 — Derive BasketId from the authenticated session

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-134](#f-134) — Basket item creation lets a duplicate BasketId target

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/basketItems.ts:42`

**How:**

1. Ignore any client-supplied BasketId and set BasketId from `user.bid`, taken from the server-side session.
2. Reject bodies that contain duplicate keys, and validate the same value that is persisted.

**Verification:** Send a POST with BasketId repeated, first the caller's id and then a foreign id. Expect 401, or the item saved to the caller's basket only.

---

<a id="m-275"></a>
#### M-275 — Scope review updates to the authenticated author

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-135](#f-135) — Review update lacks author ownership check

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:17`

**How:**

1. Return 401 if user is undefined.
2. Add author: `user.data.email` to the update selector and return 403 when nothing matches.

**Verification:** As user A, PATCH a review written by user B and confirm a 403 response and an unchanged message.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

---

<a id="m-276"></a>
#### M-276 — Bind review updates to the review author

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-136](#f-136) — Any authenticated user can edit other users'

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Add author: `user.data.email` to the update filter, alongside _id, and reject the request when no user is resolved.
2. Reject non-string ids and drop the multi option so that one request updates at most one review.

**Verification:** As user A, PATCH `/rest/products/reviews` with the id of user B's review. Expect 403 or `modified=0`, and expect B's review to be unchanged.

<a id="m-277"></a>
#### M-277 — Bind basket item access to the caller's basket

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-137](#f-137) — Basket items readable and deletable across users by id

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `server.ts:359`

**How:**

1. Add read and delete hooks or middleware that load the BasketItem and check that its BasketId equals the authenticated user's basket id.
2. Return 403 or 404 when they do not match.

**Verification:** As user A, send GET and DELETE `/api/BasketItems/`&lt;id in B's basket> and expect 403 or 404 for both.

<a id="m-278"></a>
#### M-278 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (F-138)

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-138](#f-138) — Any authenticated user can read any user record by id

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:365`

**How:**

1. Add a `resource.read.auth` or middleware hook that compares `req.params.id` with the id of the authenticated user (`authenticatedUsers.from(req)`) and returns 403 on mismatch unless the caller is an admin.
2. Exclude `deluxeToken` and `lastLoginIp` from the generated User resource output.

**Verification:** As user A, GET `/api/Users/`&lt;id of B&gt; and check for a 403 response.

<a id="m-279"></a>
#### M-279 — Reject unknown payment modes before upgrade

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-139](#f-139) — Deluxe membership granted without payment for unknown

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-841](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/841.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**How:**

1. Accept only wallet or card as `paymentMode` and return 400 for any other or missing value.
2. Update the role only after the wallet debit or card charge succeeds, using an atomic conditional balance decrement for the wallet.

**Verification:** With a valid customer token, POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` with `paymentMode`=none and with an empty body, and confirm each returns 400 with the role unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-841](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/841.html)

<a id="m-280"></a>
#### M-280 — Restrict log file access to administrators

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-140](#f-140) — Server access logs are downloadable without any authorization

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:283`

**How:**

1. Remove the public `/support/logs` routes, or put them behind `isAuthorized` plus a server-side admin role check.
2. Keep logs outside the publicly served routes and ship them to a protected log store.

**Verification:** Without a token, and with a customer token, request GET `/support/logs` and GET `/support/logs/access.log`.`<date>`. Expect 401 or 403.

<a id="m-281"></a>
#### M-281 — Reject deluxe upgrades without a verified payment

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-141](#f-141) — Deluxe membership granted without payment verification

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**How:**

1. Accept only 'wallet' or 'card' as `paymentMode` and return 400 for anything else.
2. Grant the role only after a successful charge from the wallet or card.

**Verification:** POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` with an empty body as a customer, and assert a 400 response with the role unchanged.

<a id="m-282"></a>
#### M-282 — Bind feedback UserId to the authenticated caller

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-142](#f-142) — Client-supplied UserId accepted on feedback and complaint

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:486`

**How:**

1. Before the finale create on `/api/Feedbacks` and `/api/Complaints`, register `security.appendUserId()` or overwrite `req.body.UserId` with the authenticated user's id.
2. For anonymous callers, set UserId to null or delete it from the body.
3. Reject requests whose body UserId differs from the caller's id, instead of only recording a challenge solve.

**Verification:** Send POST `/api/Feedbacks` and POST `/api/Complaints` with another user's UserId. Confirm that each stored record has the caller's id, or null for an anonymous caller, or that the request is rejected.

---

<a id="m-284"></a>
#### M-284 — Enforce coupon policy in the tool server-side

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-144](#f-144) — Chatbot coupon tool issues any model-chosen discount

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Constrain the schema with `z.number().int().min(1).max(10)` and reject values above the cap inside execute.
2. Require an authenticated, verified user and a verified damaged order owned by that user before issuing a coupon.
3. Bind issued coupons to the user and the order, and record them server-side.

**Verification:** Send POST `/rest/chat` with a prompt that makes the model request a 50% coupon. Confirm that the tool returns an error and that no coupon code above 10 is ever emitted.

<a id="m-285"></a>
#### M-285 — Enforce authorization on the server (F-145)

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-145](#f-145) — Client-side role guards trust unverified JWT claims

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-024](#w-024)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:54`

**How:**

1. Treat Angular guards as UX only: ensure every API used by administration/accounting views verifies the JWT signature and role server-side.
2. Obtain the role for UI decisions from a server-validated endpoint (e.g. whoami) rather than client-decoded claims.

**Verification:** With a customer token, call each admin/accounting API directly and confirm 401/403; with a tampered unsigned token in `localStorage`, confirm the admin view loads no data.

**Reference:** [CWE-602: Client-Side Enforcement of Server-Side Security](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

<a id="m-286"></a>
#### M-286 — Restrict user listing to the admin role

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-146](#f-146) — Missing role check on authenticated user

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/authenticatedUsers.ts:12`

**How:**

1. Add an admin role check (a verified token whose role is admin) before `retrieveUserList`.
2. Return an explicit field allowlist (id, email, `lastLoginTime`) instead of spreading `user.dataValues`.

**Verification:** Call GET `/rest/user/authentication-details` with a customer token and expect 403. Call it with an admin token and expect no `deluxeToken` field in the response.

## 11. Out of Scope

### Not Covered by This Method

An automated, AI-assisted threat model built from the repository's source code and configuration - static analysis only, no dynamic or penetration testing. It models the system as built rather than as designed. It does not replace a threat-modeling session with the team. Business context was taken from `juice-shop3/docs/business-context.md`; design intent, runtime behaviour and production configuration are not covered, and each finding still needs confirmation in the deployed system.

- Design intent and the reasoning behind it - no design documents, ADRs or workshop context are read.
- Business processes and user journeys beyond the supplied business context.
- Runtime behaviour, deployment topology and production-only configuration.
- External and organizational controls.

Treat this report as review input, not sign-off.

### Excluded from This Assessment

The following items are **explicitly excluded** from this threat model. Findings against these areas should be tracked separately.

- Third-party hosted dependencies and SaaS endpoints
- Browser runtime vulnerabilities and end-user device security
- Operating system kernel and container runtime
- Underlying network infrastructure (DNS, BGP, ISP)
- Physical security of hosting facilities
- Opt-in threat actors, not assessed: malicious insiders with repository or pipeline access; malicious insiders with production access; attackers holding a user's device. Enable them with `enable:` in `.appsec/actors.yaml`.

<a id="appendix-verification-items"></a>
## Appendix: Verification Items

Verification checks fixed security properties item by item (catalog version 6). Results per item and component in this run: 47 findings · 2 controls · 2 partial · 21 no finding · 28 undecidable · 188 not applicable. A control means that the examined code enforces the property at every place it applies; a partial, undecidable or no-finding result is never a control, and neither is an item this run did not examine.
